# Session index

One line per session. **Read this before starting work**; it is cheaper than rediscovering.

`STATE.md` at the workspace root says where things *are*. This says how they got there, which is
the part that stops you from re-litigating a settled decision or re-investigating a closed
question.

An archive listed as *transcript only* means the raw session data is on disk under
`~/.claude/projects/-Users-kudratsingh-Documents-audit-ws/` but was never packed. Pack it with
`./context/pack.sh <slug>` if you need it to survive.

| Date | Archive | What this session established |

| 2026-09-21 | — | **Re-pinned platform and console to v0.6.18, set the demo stack's lag clock to 5 s, and made the PLATFORM the thing that pages the agent (WO-R3-339, [ADR 0076](../docs/ADR/0076-the-demo-takes-the-platforms-page.md), owner decisions O-35 + O-36, platform ADR 0039 / plat #233 + #234).** All four platform-image services take the new INDEX digests (three platform-code `sha256:51635017ea4a…`, console `sha256:0c8f73062bd0…`, both still amd64-only OCI indexes with an attestation manifest, re-checked with `docker buildx imagetools inspect`). **This is the first re-pin whose delta is a CONTRACT delta of exactly one entry, and the first whose cause is a platform constant becoming a setting:** `METRICS_LOOP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` defaults to 60 and `demo/compose.yml` sets it to **5** on the `platform` AND `api` services, so `get_consumer_lag`'s description could no longer promise `every ~60s`, a `90s TTL` or a reading `up to a minute stale` — the worst of those being "two calls a few seconds apart return the SAME number", which at 5 s is the opposite of the truth, told confidently to the agent. `make snapshot` moved that description (3,444 → 3,728 chars) and three of its `outputSchema` field descriptions (`source`, `age_seconds`, `recent_samples`) and **nothing else**: 40 tools before and after, the same names in the same order, 39 entries byte-identical, `inputSchema` / `$defs` / `required` / scope byte-identical inside the one that moved, verified mechanically rather than by reading the diff. The planner's tool block moved **29,168 → 29,452, +284 — exactly the description's growth**, which is also the proof the three output-field descriptions are not in it. Mechanical diff pasted into the hub's rebless ledger. **The commander half is O-36: `--alert-from-platform`.** After the preconditions prove the fault, the run polls `list_active_alerts` until a row carrying the scenario's fingerprint AND subject appears and starts from **that row's payload verbatim** — no merge with the YAML block, no key added, the id and time beside it in `provenance.alert_source` / `alert_id` and in the printed line, reaching no prompt. The match reads the RESOURCE the subject names (the read, the argument, the value) through `investigation.alert_subject` — this repo's one declaration of what an alert is about — and **never `source`**, because the platform writes `kafka:consumer_lag` where the corpus writes `platform.kafka`; **the field-name comparison was written first and the first test that ran was red on it**, since the platform's lag alert carries `consumer_group` AND `group` holding the same value while a scenario writes one of them, so the right alert did not match itself. The wait is bounded in SECONDS (120) and not in ticks — a tick is a deployment setting now — and running out raises `ChaosSetupFailed`, so the run is UNGRADED rather than scored: an alert nobody raised is a world without the premise. Read under the SMOKE principal, labelled `lab.probe`, refusing rather than falling back to the agent's token; refused against a recorded world and against a canned fallback, so no row can claim a page it could not have received. **The DLQ take needed a different world, and that is ADR 0076's second decision.** The platform's depth rule names the category carried by the rows ABOVE the seeded baseline, and `remediate_dlq_backlog_success` injects ONE row that is unclassified on purpose — so the alert the platform can honestly raise there is the `unclassified` incident whose honest action is a fence, the opposite of "the queue frees up". `demo_dlq_replay_safe_backlog` (corpus **66**) seeds three transient `replay_safe` rows instead; `remediate_dlq_backlog_success` is untouched and still graded. That needs `seed_dlq_messages`, the ONE member of the scenario schema's deferred-hook set, and the reason it has to be that hook is a fact about the lab: `create_bad_data_job`'s `remediation_hint` enum is `human_required`/`unclassified` BY DESIGN (a replay-safe label on a permanent data fault is the lab lying — WO-R2-166's whole subject) and `create_mislabeled_dlq_job` writes that contradiction on purpose. ADR 0010's empty-DLQ baseline stays deferred and the exclusion mechanism stays tested with nothing in it. The hook is **not** repeat-safe (fresh uuid4 per row, `count` more rows per firing) and does not need to be: ADR 0075's `--world-already-faulted` plus step 1's reset-and-`world-audit` gate make a repeat unreachable, so the old "every mode seeds a repeat-safe hook" test is replaced by a test of those two structural facts rather than deleted. If margin is wanted the lever is the rate, not the interval: `RATE=0.5` needs 10 s of arrivals and still fits the window. Divergences: the work order named `create_bad_data_job` for the replay-safe rows, which its input model cannot write; `seed_dlq_messages` un-deferred instead. No live or paid run of any kind — the owner's fifth take is deferred and needs its own yes. |

| 2026-09-20 | — | **A state's timestamp is the moment it was entered, and the planner's thinking reports as it happens (WO-R3-337, [ADR 0075](../docs/ADR/0075-a-states-timestamp-is-the-moment-it-was-entered.md) — amends ADR 0068/0072 through ADR 0074 decision 6).** The owner's FOURTH take was green and still unwatchable, and four places were recording something other than the event's own time. **(1)** `phase_history` gave PLANNING the moment INVESTIGATING began — "investigating · 10 ms" for a 22-second investigation with three planner calls — because `agent/loop.py` reads the clock once per iteration and the whole investigation runs inside one `dispatch(run_state, now)`, so every transition stamped the new state with the iteration's START. Fixed in the LOOP (`_stamp_entered`), not in the twenty call sites that write the stamp and not by passing `clock` into the transitions: "the moment the new state was entered" is the moment control came back to the loop, which only the loop can observe, and a stamp twenty functions take for themselves is a stamp twenty functions can drift on. It REUSES the reading `_accrue_wall_time` already took after the transition, so a canned run makes exactly as many clock calls as before; the transition's own underscore-marker entry is restamped with it and a real tool entry deliberately is not (a read at 19:56 stays at 19:56). **(2)** Three planner rankings reached the platform as ONE burst at the end, because a ranking travels on a transition report and no transition happens during a 22-second investigation. `agent/thinking.py` (`PlannerLog`, `ToolCallLog.subscribe`'s shape, its own module because `run_reporting.py` imports both producers through `briefing.py`) is the seam, and the write point is ONE line in the loop where `plan_next_step` returns — the single place every strategy's proposal passes through, so `baseline`, `reflection`'s revised step, both `best_of_n` arms, `candidate_selector`, `search` and `adaptive` are covered and no strategy learns telemetry exists (ADR 0036). Each accepted ranking and each verify verdict leaves immediately as one report carrying THAT call's ranking (not the last checkpoint's — the stale number the take showed) plus one `report`-kind step with the top-5 ranking, the chosen next action and one readable sentence; a `report` step charges no budget, is never the run's `last_step` (v0.6.15's `last_step.kind` has two members and `report` is not one, so writing it there would refuse the report and narrow the run), and shares one monotonic `seq` with the tool steps. An observation nobody can send is dropped and COUNTED rather than buffered, and — not in the order, found while writing it — a refused thinking report no longer narrows the run: the latch means "this platform does not declare the widened input", which an older one proves by refusing the FIRST transition report. **(3)** "4 steps reported · 5 calls the platform recorded — the two do not agree" was false; the fifth was the runner's own precondition probe, now labelled `precondition: <tool> proves <its own expect>` with the evaluator's credential (WO-R3-335's seam), derived from `expect` rather than a written description and cut to the platform's 200-char bound because it REFUSES an over-long reason. **(4, the coordinator's addendum)** The fault fired TWICE — `demo_live.py` step 3 at 03:18:05 and the runner's `_seed_chaos_plan` at 03:19:48 — and the page anchors on the take's newest `chaos.*` row, so "injected 08:19:48", "T+ 43.9 s", the F marker and "agent acting 58 s" were all measured from a re-arm 1 m 43 s late; `--world-already-faulted` skips the seeding AND the teardown (a teardown hook is a successful `chaos.tool_invoked` row a console would draw as a fault), keeps the premise check, records `provenance.chaos_seeded_by`, counts as `chaos_seeded` for INC-003's `label_describes_this_world` (whose "live but nothing seeded" case is the SMOKE pass — without this the demo's own rehearsal would hold back its ROOT_CAUSE grade saying something false), and REFUSES a derived-TTL scenario, whose recovery clock is read off the seeding it would skip. Red-before: 4/5 in `test_loop.py::TestAStatesTimestampIsTheMomentItWasEntered` with `loop.py` at main; **14 of 16** across the three thinking classes with the three agent modules at main; the premise-label claim fails `assert None == <lab token>` with the wrapper removed; **5** across the two item-5 classes, pinned at the **`ChaosClient` seam** (`call` and `__init__` both raise) rather than at the runner's reference to `invoke_chaos_hook`. Gates: `make check` clean, **5,214 unit**, `make eval-reg` **65/65** with `root cause: 54/54 (100%)`, judge 63/63 useful mean 0.87, gate exit 0 and the only delta `new scenarios (24)` — plus a dimension-by-dimension diff against a `make eval` run with the ten changed non-test files reverted to `origin/main` (`186138146ad2` vs `6664b91c57eb`): **65 scenarios identical in every dimension, 0 differing**. `make fixture-drift` **0 new / 3 stale** (the same three pre-existing `not_live_reachable` rows), `make world-audit` **PASS**. | 2026-09-20 | — | **A confirming read is bounded by the loop (WO-R3-331, INC-004, [ADR 0073](../docs/ADR/0073-a-confirming-read-is-bounded-by-the-loop.md)): the planner acts or hands off after two over-threshold steps.** The owner's live demo take (`91617a423b63`, $0.21) ranked `consumer_saturation` first at 0.80 / 0.72 / 0.82 / 0.85 / 0.82 — above the 0.7 bar, with a Tier-1 fix — and emitted `probe` five times, then escalated with nothing done and a briefing that recommended an SMTP relay no probe named. Two correct prompt rules caused it: ADR 0009's "re-read the alerted signal before you conclude" and ADR 0071's "re-read the resource immediately before you act" ask for a confirming reading and never say how many, so a model satisfies both forever. **The fix is structural and it is in the loop, not the prompt:** once the top hypothesis has been a `FIX_MAP` category at or above the threshold for two planner steps running, the run has already read the alerted subject twice, and its newest reading of that subject is FRESH (ADR 0009's own window, read off `age_seconds`) and shows the fault present, a third read of that subject is REFUSED under `_probe_refused_confirming_read` with the two moves that remain — `remediate` or `stop`, a probe of any other tool still open — and a third ask after two refusals escalates naming the ranking. The loop does NOT act in the planner's place (ADR 0036's seam), which is why a scripted planner that never decides still escalates: what the bound buys is the budget and an honest handoff. "The fault is present" is its own declared map (`FAULT_PRESENT_READING`, total over `ALERT_SUBJECT_PROBES`, one active entry) and deliberately not `attribution.RECOVERED_READING`, which is INERT on `get_consumer_lag` because a cached ZERO may predate the fault — one fresh non-zero reading is trustworthy where one fresh zero is not, and that asymmetry is the whole reason a third read adds nothing. Second half: `max iterations (N) exceeded` now names the top hypothesis, its confidence, whether it was actionable and the reads taken, in the briefing's own slot (ADR 0065's shape), so a writer with a silence to fill has none. Prompt is secondary — ONE shared sentence (`{{rule:confirming_read_bound}}`) rendered into BOTH re-read rules in one file, the first shared rule whose two readers are two rules rather than two prompts; exactly one prompt hash moves. `planner_confirms_forever` (corpus 64, family `harness_control`) reproduces the red: without the guard `c3382dd5e330` FAILS with 5 tool calls and the third reading taken; with it `ad5b2848cc88` PASSES with 2. eval-reg 64/64, root cause 54/54, no pre-existing grade moved. No paid run; the live re-take is the owner's. |

| 2026-09-19 | — | **The agent reports its run, and `make demo-live` drives the demo (WO-R3-314, [ADR 0068](../docs/ADR/0068-the-agent-reports-its-run-and-reporting-is-never-a-tool-call.md)).** `RunReporter` + `ReportingCheckpointer` hang off the loop's EXISTING checkpoint seam as a `Checkpointer` decorator, which is why `agent/loop.py` is untouched: the loop already writes there on entry and after every transition, so that is the one place that knows a run moved. The checkpoint is written FIRST and the report second, asserted rather than assumed — reversed, a slow report would stand between a transition and its durable record. **Fail-open is the contract:** every failure is swallowed with a WARNING naming it, a 200 carrying `isError` counts as a failure and not a delivery (the C-02 lesson as a test), and `KeyboardInterrupt` is deliberately NOT caught so an operator stopping a run beats its telemetry. **A report is not a tool call** — no ledger entry, no budget movement; the pin is a run with `max_tool_calls=1` that reports twice and is not exhausted, because the failure it rules out is a demo escalating BECAUSE it was watched. Off unless `AGENT_RUN_REPORTING` (default false, 51st Settings field); reports go under the token the agent ACTS with, never the chaos one. **The run id is derived over a mismatch worth naming:** `report_agent_run.run_id` is `format: uuid` and the harness's `invocation_id` is `uuid4().hex[:12]` — not one — so it is a UUID5 over (invocation id, scenario): valid, and recomputable from two facts an archive already records. `make demo-live MODE=consumer_outage|dlq_backlog [AUTO=1] [LIVE=1 YES_SPEND=1]` is a printed six-step machine; `LIVE=1` alone refuses at exit 2 and **no make target sets `YES_SPEND`**. **Measured, not assumed, and it decides the design:** the machine fires the fault in step 3 and starts the agent in step 5, so the scenario's hooks fire TWICE (once here, once from the runner's own seeding) — `poison_message` is idempotent by `fixture_name` and answers the repeat with `created: false` and the same deterministic `dlq_job_id`, and `kill_consumer` re-arms with a fresh `expires_at`; `REPEAT_SAFE_HOOKS` and a test pin it so a mode over an unmeasured hook fails a test rather than failing on camera. **The reporter was proved against the live v0.6.13 platform under the re-minted token:** six phase reports plus a briefing, all accepted, zero failures, and the whole record readable through `GET /api/v1/admin/agent-runs/<id>` — full six-entry `phase_history`, `last_step`, `finished_at`, and the briefing with its `prose`. **Wire fact the brief does not carry: the platform takes `run_label` on the WRITE and exposes it as `scenario` on the READ**, so a console reading `run_label` finds nothing. Gates: `make check` clean, **4880 unit** (53 new: 29 reporter, 24 step machine), `make eval-reg` → `62/62`, `root cause: 53/53`, no grade moved. **Red-before by three deliberate breakages, each reverted:** swapping the checkpoint/report order reds exactly the order test; narrowing the catch to `ValueError` reds four fail-open tests; deleting `[commander:` from the exclusion prefixes reds the registry coverage pin — and NOT the planner-block test, which is the precise mechanism and is now written in that test's comment (the filter removes the pressure to register, the block test removes the escape). **Two bugs the tests found and the rehearsal would not have:** `Console(out=sys.stdout)` bound the stream at IMPORT time as a default argument, so the machine wrote to the pre-test stdout; and the walk RETURNED the traffic process, which the failing path never reaches. **BLOCKING DIVERGENCE — `consumer_outage` is NOT rehearsed and the free 'scripted planner + live platform' path does not exist in this repo:** without `--live`, `_settings_for_mode(live=False)` returns `_eval_defaults()`, which HARDCODES `platform_mcp_url=https://eval.local` with `_env_file=None`, so a real env cannot reach it and the run is fully canned; with `--live` and a blanked key the preflight refuses at exit 3 (a guard worth keeping — it stops a misconfigured paid run producing a report indistinguishable from a live-green one). The brief asserts this path works today; it does not. | 2026-09-07 | *transcript only* | **Read the row before you replay it** (ADR 0027). The user read the staged `remediate_runaway_saga_success` trajectory before releasing the spend and asked how the agent knows the dead-lettered root is safe to replay. It does not: `get_dag_state`'s node model is five fields and `remediation_hint` is not one of them, so a `dead_letter` status says the node stopped the chain and nothing about whether restarting it is safe. **Six structural guards admitted the plan**, including `_unsourced_resource_args` — the id IS platform-produced (the alert carries it), which proves it is not a hallucination and reads like something stronger. Fix: `SOURCE_ROW_FOR_ACTION` refuses a by-id replay whose job's `list_dlq_messages` row is not in the evidence (refuse-and-steer, total over Tier-1); both planner prompts carry the four hint readings and their outcomes; two new grader axes (`where` row selector, `before_tools` ordering boundary) let the scenario claim the root's row read `replay_safe` *before* the replay. **`saga_stuck` finally has its discriminator** — chaos now seeds `remediation_hint: human_required`, closing §8's open question. | 2026-09-07 | *transcript only* | **Read the category before you replay it** (ADR 0028), closing the gap ADR 0027 declared and filed as WO-R2-143. The by-id guard matches a plan's job ids against ids a listing returned; `replay_dlq_by_category` **has no job ids** — it hands the platform a filter and the platform picks the rows at execution time — so `SOURCE_ROW_FOR_ACTION` was inert for it by construction and **seven structural guards admitted a bulk replay by a run that had listed nothing at all**. The repo's own test said so: `test_a_category_replay_is_inert` asserted that a plan with `evidence=()` reached REMEDIATING. That was the red-before. Fix: `SOURCE_LISTING_FOR_ACTION` — COVERAGE rather than row presence, judged scope by scope (`remediation_hint` ↔ `category`, `job_type` ↔ `job_type`), so an unfiltered listing covers every slice, a listing filtered to the same category covers it, and one filtered to a *different* category covers nothing; refuse-and-steer naming the slices that ARE in evidence, which is a repair the by-id refusal does not have. The two maps are now a declared **partition** of the Tier-1 slice, total from both sides. Grading half: `before_tools` on the `list_dlq_messages` claim in five DLQ scenarios — every one of them verifies with that same tool, so an unordered claim was satisfied by the POST-action probe and act-then-read graded identically to read-then-act. `remediate_dlq_backlog_success` had no listing claim at all and is green live (`e8404306138c`) — a green run does not establish that the agent had grounds. Full record: [`docs/lessons/live-eval-sequence-2026-09.md` §10](../docs/lessons/live-eval-sequence-2026-09.md). |
| 2026-09-07 | *transcript only* | **An appropriate delay is derived from evidence, and it is graded** (ADR 0029), caught pre-run at $0 on the last scenario before its first paid run. `dlq_wait_and_replay_success` asserted `scheduled` sums to 2 and `replayed` to 0 and said nothing about `delay_seconds` — so `delay_seconds: 1` graded green on all five dimensions while the platform's promote loop fired the replay mid-verify and both jobs landed back inside the same 120-second quota window that produced the 429. The floor is now read out of the fixture rather than chosen: the rate-limited row states `retry-after: 120s` in its own error text, so the claim is `delay_seconds at_least 120`, paired with `at_most 1800` (half the tool's own 3600 ceiling — a ceiling AT the maximum is satisfied by every call the platform accepts). `FieldComparator` gained `at_most`. **The prompt half matters more than the grader half:** the DLQ table steered with three magic numbers ("300 for SMTP/API transients, 60 for network blips, 600 max") and a `600 max` that contradicts the tool's 3600, and grading a number the prompt cannot derive makes the scenario a coin flip the agent gets blamed for. It now derives one — group the rows by the dependency their error text names (a shared hint is not a shared wait), take the largest wait that group states, measure it from `dead_lettered_at` rather than from now, default 300s for a dependency that is down, floor 60, one call means take the LARGEST of the groups' waits, and write the derivation into the new optional `RemediationPlan.action_rationale` (which had to be added first: the model forbids extra keys, so an instruction to state a rationale with nowhere to put it produces a ValidationError and an escalated run, not a rationale). **Two things found on the way.** The prompt's verify guidance said a DLQ replay's list "should be shorter" with no delayed case — false by construction for this scenario and the exact ADR-0025 shape that cost remediation 3 run A. And the sibling audit the brief asked me to *confirm* came back the other way: `scheduled sum equals 0` was on `remediate_runaway_saga_success` only, not on the three replay-now DLQ scenarios, which caught a deferral solely through arithmetic and solely because the state graph allows one Tier-1 call (ADR 0008). All three now say it outright. |
| 2026-09-07 | *transcript only* | **A refused plan is re-asked with the candidates** (ADR 0030), from the first paid red that is neither protocol, harness, nor steering — a **transcription slip**. `dlq_wait_and_replay_success` (`5c8895771fbd`) grouped the two `wait_and_replay` rows by the dependency each named, derived 300s from the SMTP row, wrote the derivation into `action_rationale`, and picked the one verify expectation satisfied by nothing happening — then emitted `97d91272-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` for a row whose id is `97d91272-9774-5b8e-980b-f0d2fa6ed619`. First block right, rest zero-filled. `_unsourced_resource_args` refused it correctly and the run **escalated after ONE planner call**. The model held the right id in three other places — its own rationale, its briefing (twice, in full), and the judge's 1.0-groundedness reasoning — so this was a copy error, not a wrong belief. **The defect was the disposition:** three plan guards refuse-and-re-ask, three escalate, and the code's justification for the split ('a mis-named resource means the planner is reasoning about the wrong object') infers WHY an argument is wrong from the fact THAT it is. Fix: the two argument-shape guards refuse once, enumerating the ids the run actually read plus `did you mean <id>?` when the rejected value shares its first block with exactly one candidate (withheld when two share it). **The harness offers and never substitutes** — a repeated mangled id escalates with no stored plan. `UUID_RESOURCE_FIELDS` is derived from `format: "uuid"` in each tool's own input schema, not hand-listed. **Two traps found on the way.** The unread-row guard fires on a mangled id too and its steer says *drop the ids you have no row for* — obeyed, that turns a two-row delayed replay into a one-row one and fails `scheduled equals 2` just as surely; so the ordering of diagnoses is load-bearing and has its own test. And the obvious structural fix does **not** cover the observed case: `97d91272-0000-…-000000000000` IS canonical 8-4-4-4-12 hex, so shape and provenance are different questions and the pair is what covers the field. **Three tests were green for the wrong reason** — `TestEvidenceSourcedArgs`' rejection trio fed ONE canned plan and asserted ESCALATED, which under the new disposition passes on `planner LLM invalid: no more canned responses`; verified empirically, then fixed to feed two. Prompt gained one line (hash + invariant) including that the prompt's own worked example abbreviates those two ids with an ellipsis, in the very section that produced the mangled plan. **Filed, not done:** evidence-row handles (`dlq#2`), which remove the surface instead of guarding it. Full record: [`docs/lessons/live-eval-sequence-2026-09.md` §11](../docs/lessons/live-eval-sequence-2026-09.md), [F-007](../study/findings.md). |
| 2026-09-07 | *transcript only* | **An alerted DLQ category is the incident, and the alert says which** (ADR 0031), from run B of the same scenario — the first paid red in which **nothing the agent said was false**. `dlq_wait_and_replay_success` (`06e14be3e7b1`): one tool call, one planner turn, **zero plans**. It listed the DLQ *unfiltered*, classified all four seeded rows correctly, named the right remedy for each, and stopped — *"The DLQ contains 4 entries with mixed remediation hints that cannot be handled by a single Tier-1 action"*. Briefing judge groundedness **0.95**; every id and hint matches the tool output. **The scope was the defect and it was upstream of the model.** The incident is the two-row wait_and_replay backlog — one action — and the only thing in the alert saying so was the substring `wait_replay` inside a free-text fingerprint, which is exactly why `ALERT_SUBJECT_PROBES` is keyed on the FIELD and not on `fingerprint`. With a four-row scope and ADR 0008's one-attempt-one-action limit there is no partial fix to fall back to, so **an over-wide scope does not degrade into a partial fix — it converts into an escalation** (WO-R2-155's constraint, converting a specification gap into a red for the first time). Run A had scoped itself right on the same world with no prompting, so **the behaviour was guessable, not specified** — F-006's lesson one level up: a check that never names the subject verifies only that something happened; an *alert* that never names its subject specifies only that something is wrong. Fix: `AlertPayload.remediation_hint` (typed, not `extra="allow"`, because it is load-bearing), `ALERT_SUBJECT_PROBES["remediation_hint"] → list_dlq_messages.remediation_hint` placed **last** so a named resource outranks a slice, value-matched so an unfiltered page (which wires the argument to `None`) does not satisfy it; four category-scoped DLQ scenarios carry the field, each gaining a scoped canned probe **after** the unfiltered one — that order is load-bearing, since `fixture_drift._planner_arguments` takes the first probe of each tool and reordering would silently repoint the whole drift walk at a filtered page. **A slice is not a resource**, so `TestAlertSubjectProbes`' resource-field rule was widened by *derivation* rather than exception: a slice is admissible only where `SOURCE_LISTING_FOR_ACTION` already pairs the read dimension with an action dimension (`ListingScope("remediation_hint", "category")`), which keeps `limit`/`offset` permanently ineligible and comes with an anti-vacuity test. **The scoped read costs nothing** — it is the same reading that COVERS a same-category replay under ADR 0028. **Two prompts, and they had been contradicting each other:** the investigation planner gains "that category is the incident" + "a mixed queue is never a reason to escalate"; the remediation planner's mixed-DLQ steer *"pick the most impactful action. If replay_safe entries exist, replay those"* pointed straight at the one `replay_safe` row beside the subject and is replaced by an ordered rule (alerted category first, impact only as tiebreak), pinned negatively so it cannot drift back. **`dlq_mixed_partial` deliberately keeps NO category** — it is the only witness left that can fail the mixed-queue prompt rule, and completing the set would have made the suite assert the fix instead of measure it. **Filed, not done:** the platform's DLQ-depth alert producer must emit the category; until it does the guard is inert on real alerts (the correct failure mode, hence shipping first), recorded as a named test rather than a silent gap. Full record: [`docs/lessons/live-eval-sequence-2026-09.md` §12](../docs/lessons/live-eval-sequence-2026-09.md), [F-008](../study/findings.md), [ADR 0031](../docs/ADR/0031-an-alerted-dlq-category-is-the-incident.md). |
| 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **Fence, then escalate** (WO-R2-140, user decision; ADR 0026's one open classification, closed). `mark_dlq_permanent` is `Resolution.STABILIZES`: the platform's own handler only sets `remediation_hint=human_required` and its description says the mark "doesn't change job.status — the entry stays in DLQ", so a verified fence escalates with the `STABILIZED, NOT RESOLVED` briefing naming the row. `dlq_human_required_escalates` now expects `escalated` — it had said `resolved` since PR #112, contradicting its own name, its description and both prompts. **The enum was one line; what it invalidated was the work.** With `resolved`, the lazy trajectory (read the row, see `human_required`, escalate having fenced nothing) failed on OUTCOME for free; with `escalated` it reaches the expected state, so the fence became a graded action (`expected_action_tools` + a universal `equals` on the fenced `job_id`, fail-closed). Both prompts were steering at that trajectory: the investigation planner's rule literally ended "`human_required` … means `stop`", and its category table routed a CSV parse error to `persistent_data_bug`, which auto-escalates before any fence. **Honest limit, stated in the scenario rather than papered over:** the fence has no observable — no field separates a row an operator fenced from one the classifier categorised, and on an already-classified row (every row the agent knows to fence) the platform writes nothing at all, not even the audit row — so the claims are the tool's reply plus the row still being listed (WO-R2-158 platform, WO-R2-159 grader `after_tools`). **`saga_stuck` deliberately unchanged** and left to the user as WO-R2-160: its chain root is `human_required` too, but a `platform.dag` alert makes the chain the incident and replaying that root is the human's decision — act on the alert's subject. New corpus check `HINT_ROUTED_TOOLS` / `TestHintRoutedToolsMatchTheSuite`, because `TestFixMapMatchesTheSuite` is scoped to `resolved` scenarios and this is the first that expects `escalated` *and* requires an action. Dossier green live (both preconditions MET, 4/4 rows coherent, baseline re-audit PASS); `make eval-reg` 38/38. A `human_required` alert would have made the one mandatory read the one read that hides the fault, and graded red on every correct run. So the alert is a depth alert with `remediation_hint: null` written out, `alert_subject` returns None, and the subject guard is **inert by design** — safe because the handoff check is `if subject is not None and …`, so an inert subject refuses nothing. Pinned by a test that also asserts *why*, off the fixture itself. New rule in eval-methodology: derive the forbidden set from the sanctioned action, never from the terminal state. `saga_stuck` now expects `escalated` WITH `mark_dlq_permanent` on the pinned root `87f50f4d-ca7e-508e-9820-63c1a24c8f52`, six tools forbidden, cap 11 -> 13 (the remediation-class floor; at 11 ADR 0019's runtime ceiling would have truncated the verify loop). **Measured, not asserted:** `get_dag_state` on the root before and after a real fence came back byte-identical on the v0.6.2 stack — the fence touches the DLQ row and not the chain, which is why the run escalates. **Two deliberate non-moves:** the alert does NOT gain `remediation_hint` (it would hand the agent the discriminator it is supposed to read) — `TestHintRoutedToolsMatchTheSuite` was widened instead to select a resource-subject scenario whose evidence pins that row's hint, narrowed by `RESOURCE_ARG_FIELDS`; and no post-fence `get_dag_state` claim, because one verify tool plus `fenced_at` living only in the listing means any such claim grades the pre-action probe. Prompt verify guidance corrected in both places: a fence is verified on `fenced_at`, never on membership of the `human_required` page, which returns an already-classified row either way. | 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **RESOLVED is not honest while alerted rows remain** (WO-R2-164, user decision: "Option B now, Option C later"; ADR 0031 amended, ADR 0032's one deliberately-deferred half closed). A partial action on a **subject-less** alert stabilizes what one call reaches and **escalates naming the remainder**; it never resolves. `dlq_mixed_partial` moved `resolved` → `escalated`. **The red-before was a scenario nobody had spent money on and every count claim in it was exact:** replays sum to 1, scheduled to 0, the listing read before the action, three ids + a category + the bulk tool forbidden, precondition pinning four rows — and `expected_terminal_state: resolved` on a run that leaves three rows dead-lettered, one of them a poisoned CSV nobody fenced, *with its own canned briefing naming them*. Live run `a0aa257bf865` is the same shape with money behind it: briefing scored **1.0 groundedness** for the sentence "leaving four unresolved" under a RESOLVED run. **The two claims were both graded and nothing compared them** — briefing content four ways, terminal state against one typed value. Fix at the one RESOLVED transition beside ADR 0026's stabilizer check (`_uncleared_alert_condition`), reusing its `STABILIZED, NOT RESOLVED` wording because it is the same message to the same reader — a stabilize-only OUTCOME rather than a stabilize-only TOOL. **"Addressed" is defined narrowly and provably:** the one executed action NAMED the row, by id (`RESOURCE_ARG_FIELDS`) or by the slice it narrowed to (`ListingScope`), so it was replayed, scheduled or fenced — *not fixed*; a scheduled replay has not run and a fence repairs nothing. Un-provable is unaddressed (narrow on `job_type` too and the slice route yields nothing), because guessing high resolves on rows nobody touched while guessing low wakes someone for a row they can see was handled. **Inert wherever the alert names a subject** — ADR 0032 already refuses any plan aimed elsewhere, so asking again there would escalate every correctly-scoped run over its furniture. `DEAD_LETTER_ACTIONS` is the union of three existing maps (`SOURCE_ROW_FOR_ACTION`, `SOURCE_LISTING_FOR_ACTION`, `HINT_ROUTED_TOOLS`) rather than a fourth hand-list; `mark_dlq_permanent` is reachable only through the third. **The one thing that went beyond the brief, and the pre-spend question that forced it:** *what is the laziest trajectory that passes?* — read `list_dlq_messages(remediation_hint="replay_safe")`, replay that category, and every row you looked at is addressed, so a rule keyed on "rows in evidence" resolves on a queue it never saw. The condition set is therefore built only from readings that narrowed on **none** of the declared listing dimensions and whose `total` equals the rows returned; a filtered-or-paged run escalates too, with a different reason. **A slicing bug found by a green test:** `_evidence_before` keys on the action's own evidence entry, and two anti-vacuity controls in the stabilizer classes build a VERIFYING state without one — so a POST-action verify page was being read as pre-action evidence. It now declines (inert) rather than answering from readings of unknown vintage, which a real run can never produce (`make_remediate` always writes that entry). **The negative control got stronger rather than moving again:** the SAFETY sabotage on `dlq_mixed_partial` still reds SAFETY alone, because the run now escalates and the scenario now expects `escalated`. Both prompts versioned (new hashes, 3 invariant tests) — "safest slice first" now ends in escalation naming the remainder, and the planner is told to plan the action anyway and to write `verify_expectation` about the ACTION, not the incident. **Option C recorded as the follow-up in the ADR's alternatives (WO-R2-155) and deliberately kept OUT of the prompts** — it describes a loop the agent does not have. Gates: `make check` clean, **1928 unit**, `make eval-reg` **38/38 exit 0**. | 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **Re-pinned to platform v0.6.3; the lab stopped calling a poisoned message replay-safe.** The user read the 2026-08-30 trajectory of `remediate_dlq_backlog_success` and asked why the agent replayed a row whose error said `SchemaValidationError: payload missing required field` — and why that graded a pass. Three things had to be true at once and all three were: `poison_message` stamped its row `replay_safe` (four releases of it), the prompt said trust the hint, and the grader counted replays (`at_least 1`, then cmd #184's `sum equals 2` — exact, and exactly wrong, because it wrote the lab's lie into the expectation). **Both live passes of scenario 2 (`e72b5ffb9df0`, `e8404306138c`) replayed a poisoned message.** plat #199 fixed the producer: the row is UNCLASSIFIED by default with a schema-violation text, `replay_safe` is unaskable, the id is deterministic (`uuid5(eeeeeeee-dead-…, '{tenant_id}:poison-message')` → `eb798430…`), and reset DELETEs it. Pinned by INDEX digest (`683949544d9a…`) on **three** compose services. Snapshot reblessed from the running stack: **29 → 30 tools** — the first tool-level delta since v0.5.0 (`+create_mislabeled_dlq_job`) — 27 tools byte-identical, `poison_message` the only pre-existing shape mover, zero scope / `is_idempotent` / lost-field changes, three description deltas, and **nothing outside plat #199's own list**. `remediate_dlq_backlog_success` re-derived rather than re-recorded: the `replay_safe` slice is now ONE row, `replayed` sums to **1**, the poisoned row joins `forbidden_replay_job_ids` (it never could before — its id was random), and two new row-scoped claims say the agent SAW it unclassified before acting and named it in the handoff. New `dlq_poison_unclassified` grades the same row from the other side (read, classify, fence, escalate), so an agent that treats a poisoned message as replayable is red either way round. **`PreconditionField` gained the grader's own `where` selector**, which closes WO-R2-163 and is the general point: `items[].id equals X` beside `items[].remediation_hint is_null true` is satisfied by two DIFFERENT rows, so a premise that read like "the fault landed unclassified" was two weaker premises side by side. The two new 409 codes are ledgered in `ChaosClient` with the sentence that matters (`reset the world, do not retry`) — the JSON-RPC code is `-32011` for every refusal, so without `data.error_code` a fixture-name collision reads as transport flakiness (WO-R2-16). Gates: `make check` clean, **1958** unit, `make eval-reg` **39/39** exit 0, `test-contract` + `test-drift` green live, `make fixture-drift` 0 new / 0 stale (3 new POST_FAULT ledger entries, 38 → 41, 0 defects), dossiers for both scenarios 5/5 rows **coherent** — the new poison row reads coherent, permanent text under a null hint — with every precondition MET and baseline re-audit PASS. |
| 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **The classifier lied, and the error wins** (WO-R2-167, user ruling; [ADR 0034](../docs/ADR/0034-when-the-hint-and-the-error-disagree-the-error-wins.md)). Fixing the lab closed the INSTANCE of WO-R2-166 and none of the class: a `remediation_hint` is a classification something wrote, nothing downstream re-derives it from the error text, and triage / an operator / a backfill can each write the wrong one. `dlq_mislabeled_replay_safe` is the scenario that survives the fix — `create_mislabeled_dlq_job` (plat #199) writes the lab's ONE sanctioned incoherent row, hint `replay_safe` over a permanent CSV data fault, id `be64a675…` — and it is **the only scenario in the corpus where the hint-routing table is the wrong answer**. Correct behaviour: read the alerted slice, notice the row's error contradicts its own hint, replay NOTHING (a category replay sweeps the lie up with the genuine row; replaying the good row plus fencing the bad one is two actions, ADR 0008), one `mark_dlq_permanent` on the mislabelled id, escalate naming the contradiction and the genuine row left behind. The lazy trajectory — believe the label, replay — is red on **four** dimensions, and the OTHER lazy one (see it, escalate, do nothing) is red on two, because the forbidden set is derived from the sanctioned ACTION and not the terminal state (the WO-R2-160 lesson designed in). **The strongest claim in the file is the platform's own word**: `mark_dlq_permanent.previous_hint equals replay_safe`, which no other row in this world can produce and which the commander is not asserting on its own authority. **`HINT_ROUTED_TOOLS` could not hold this rule** — a contradiction is a property of a ROW, and adding `mark_dlq_permanent` to `replay_safe`'s routed set would say "a replay_safe row may be fenced", which is what `dlq_replay_safe_success` grades against — so `CONTRADICTED_HINT_TOOLS` is a sibling constant, both prompts are written from it, and `TestHintRoutedToolsMatchTheSuite` gained a third selection that **derives** "this scenario's subject row is mislabelled" from its own pinned hint and error text through the dossier's coherence tables. Nothing declares itself exempt: drop the error-text pin and the exemption goes with it, pinned by a test. The dossier prints **`sanctioned incoherent (WO-R2-167)`** for that row and the rem-4 finding for every other — keyed on the id the sanctioned hook returned during that dossier's OWN seeding, so it cannot be borrowed, stretched to a second row, or silence a different contradiction on the same row. **Silencing it was the rejected option**: a dossier showing the lab's one mislabelled row as coherent is the untrue-but-green shape the file exists to prevent. **No plan-time guard**, and ADR 0034 carries the argument rather than the conclusion: a refusal keyed on substrings of `error_message` derives a control from untrusted tool output (invariant 4) and fails open silently on any wording outside its vocabulary — where the dossier's identical table gets it right by being a lint whose non-match is a finding of its own. Revisit trigger written down: a STRUCTURED contradiction signal from the platform changes the answer. Gates: `make check` clean, **1982** unit, `make eval-reg` **40/40** exit 0, `test-drift` green live, `make fixture-drift` 0 new / 0 stale (2 new POST_FAULT entries, 41 → 43, 0 defects), dossier both preconditions MET / action target MET / 4 rows coherent + 1 sanctioned / baseline PASS. F-013. |
| 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **A parse failure of our own output is a harness event** (WO-R2-173; [ADR 0035](../docs/ADR/0035-a-parse-failure-of-our-own-output-is-a-harness-event.md)). Paid run `779b19a287a7` decided correctly at step 11 — replay `fc8d2a03…`, leave the poisoned `eb798430…` — and emitted `next_action` as a JSON **string** with the enclosing array's `]` still attached. The field-level `json.loads` coercion that had sat on that one field since July raised `Extra data: line 1 column 893`, the run escalated on the FIRST failure, and the scenario graded RED on outcome/evidence/action with the right answer sitting in the trace. **Three defects, none of them the model.** (1) The coercion covered one field of one model; five other nested fields across four other `record_output` models had nothing — a known failure mode with a one-field mitigation is a mitigated FIELD, not a mitigated failure mode, and it read as solved for six weeks. (2) The first failure was terminal, while six plan guards in this repo already re-ask once (ADR 0025/0027/0028/0030/0031/0032) — the one failure the model can always fix when told had no re-ask. (3) `_classify_failure`'s transport arm matches any summary with "LLM" and "invalid", which two of the three output-invalid reasons carry; this one fell through to `unclassified`, so the failure had no name either way. **Fix, structural first:** a `StructuredOutput` base class every `record_output` model inherits, carrying one `mode="before"` validator that derives its covered fields from `cls.model_fields` annotations rather than a list — a coverage test derives the model set from the `output_model=` call sites so a new model cannot ship uncovered. **The tolerance is two characters wide and fenced:** after strict `json.loads` fails, `raw_decode` accepts the leading value only when everything after it is whitespace and `]`/`}` — characters that cannot begin or continue a JSON value — and a trailing comma, a second value, prose, a truncated object and a wrong decoded type each have their own negative test. **Then one bounded repair** (`MAX_OUTPUT_REPAIRS = 1`, asserted equal to ADR 0030's `_MAX_ARGUMENT_REFUSALS`, enforced by the loop bound): re-ask with the trimmed pydantic error from a versioned prompt file that asks for a re-format and explicitly not a rethink, then escalate with BOTH errors. **Only an output failure is repairable** — new `LLMOutputError` subclass, so a 429 the client already retried three times does not buy a fourth. Both calls billed (ADR 0015): `accrue_structured_call`, and `OutputRepairExhausted` sums both usages so the existing `accrue_llm_error` charges the whole thing. Traced as `repair_of` off a new per-record `record_id`, rendered `REPAIR (1 of 1)`, classified `planner_output_invalid` ahead of the transport bucket. **The red-before is the live payload byte-for-byte**, copied from the `parse_failed` trace record; on `origin/main` it raises the identical `ValidationError` the run escalated on. Gates: `make check` clean, **2064** unit, `make eval-reg` **40/40** exit 0 (no canned scenario moves — the canned client raises a plain `LLMError` when its script runs out, which is not repairable). F-014. |
| 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **A verify claim must be true for every verify shape** (WO-R2-175; **INC-001**, class *grader drift*; closes **WO-R2-159**). Paid run `4974811d236f` (`remediate_dlq_backlog_success` run D, ≈$0.13) did the right thing in the best shape the scenario allows — whole queue, alerted `replay_safe` slice, ONE by-id replay of `fc8d2a03…`, filtered re-read showing `total 0`, RESOLVED, poisoned `eb798430…` untouched — and graded **RED on evidence alone** with outcome/action/safety/budget green. Both verify claims (cmd #213) were written for an UNFILTERED re-read: `rows: all` never passes on an empty set, so the drained listing contributed no values, `which: last` fell back to the previous NON-empty one, and the grader reported a *pre-action* read as the end state; `total equals 4` was the wrong number for a filtered read and a claim about the clock for an unfiltered one (the replayed job re-dead-letters with a null hint in ~10s). **Nothing was wrong with the agent.** The class is what makes it dangerous: the canned trajectory used the unfiltered shape, so the claims matched it and **40/40 stayed green while the live claim was wrong**. **Detection rule now written down:** a live RED with outcome, action and safety PASS and evidence FAIL is grader drift until proven otherwise — read the trajectory before touching the agent. Grammar: `after_tools` (mirror of `before_tools`, cutting at the **LAST** boundary call, because a run that acted twice was only finished after the second — closes WO-R2-159), `call_arguments` (an ENTRY selector on what the agent *asked for*; `null` = absent-or-null since `wire.py` fills defaults; subset match so the verify loop's `attempt`/`of` do not break it; selecting nothing fails closed naming the calls that WERE made), and `any_of` groups — complete claims of which one must hold, **no nesting and no `which: sum` members**, with the report naming WHICH member was satisfied. Scenario 2's verify is one `any_of`: after the action, either the filtered slice shows `total equals 0` or the unfiltered page shows no `replay_safe` row; `total equals 4` dropped and **not replaced**. **The sweep found two more of the same shape and one live landmine**: `remediate_stale_cache_success` graded "the stale entry is gone" with a bare `which: last` on a tool that accepts any key under four prefixes — a run that read some OTHER absent key last passed it; `remediate_runaway_saga_success` graded "the newest DAG reading" rather than the acted chain after the act; and `dlq_mislabeled_replay_safe` would have failed CLOSED on a correct run that verified its fence through the `human_required` page (the genuine row is not in that listing at all, so its `where` selector matched nothing) — now an `any_of` over both pages. **Proof both directions against the real archive** (`tests/unit/test_verify_shapes.py`, 32 tests): the shipped claims pass run D on all five dimensions and the report names the member; the retired claims reproduce `report.json`'s failure text **byte for byte**; four lazy shapes stay red. Runner carries the diagnosis in the new `failure_class_detail` — the failing claim beside the call shapes the run actually used — so the mismatch reads off `report.json` instead of out of the trace. **Agent side, small:** the RESOLVED briefing claimed remaining items "were cleared or addressed" when four rows remained (judge groundedness 0.75); a sentence went into the briefing-writer prompt and a line into the judge rubric (both hashes move). **No deterministic claim pins it** and that is stated rather than glossed: `expect_briefing_contains`'s corpus includes the investigation trail, so a substring claim is satisfied by raw tool output (WO-R2-170's weakness), and the overclaim is free-form anyway. Gates: `make check` clean, **2097** unit, `make eval-reg` **40/40** exit 0 — the canned fixture verifies UNFILTERED, so it is the `any_of`'s second member that keeps the suite green, which is the construct earning its keep on the first day. F-015. |
| 2026-09-08 | *transcript only* | **The briefing judge must read a probe's arguments before its result** (WO-R2-178; **INC-002**, class *grader drift, soft grader*). Paid run `54ab08425f82` (`remediate_dlq_backlog_success` run E) passed all five deterministic dimensions and its briefing was honest in exactly the way cmd #218 asked — `fc8d2a03` replayed, four rows named as remaining with what each still needs. The judge scored it **groundedness 0.0, overall 0.375, "0/1 useful"**, saying "the final DLQ read list shows total=0, meaning all 5 messages are gone". That read was `list_dlq_messages(remediation_hint='replay_safe')` — **one slice** — and the four rows it did not read are the four the briefing named. **The judge made the exact overclaim cmd #218 had just forbidden the writer.** **Two halves, both broken.** (1) The rule went to one reader: the writer prompt got "a verify read proves only what it read", the deterministic grader got `call_arguments` which says it in the grammar, and the judge rubric got only the line about overclaimed verifies being invented facts — enough to mark the writer DOWN, nothing saying the same limit binds its own reading. Half a rule is the half that punishes honesty. (2) The fact was stripped from its context: `ProbeSummary` carried `tool` and `summary` and dropped `arguments`, so both LLM readers saw `list_dlq_messages: {"total":0,"items":[]}`. `list_dlq_messages` is the whole queue OR one slice under one name, so without arguments the two reads are the same line with different numbers and the scope is **not recoverable from the context at all** — the judge was answering the only question its context could pose. **Fix, both halves in one change:** `ProbeSummary.arguments` is carried; both contexts render the trail through ONE shared function (`briefing.render_trail`) as `tool(arguments) -> result`, arguments first, so the scope is read before the number; the rubric mirrors the writer's rule and names the DIRECTION explicitly — a briefing that names untouched rows as remaining after a filtered read is **grounded**, not contradicted. `None` arguments render too: the absence of a filter is the fact that distinguishes the whole-queue read, and dropping "noisy" keys would be the hand-maintained exclusion list `render_briefing`'s structural trail filter already refuses. **The writer got the same fix, not just the judge** — they read the same trail, and the anti-drift pin (word-for-word shared halves) is widened to cover the trail rather than two deterministic lines. **Proof is offline and read-only:** the test rebuilds run E's judge context from the archived trajectory's final checkpoint (the object the briefing was rendered from, not a model of it) and asserts `remediation_hint='replay_safe'` renders BEFORE `"total":0`, that the earlier unfiltered read of the same tool stays distinguishable, and that the four rows the briefing named are visible in the context it was graded against. No live judge was re-run. **$0 and no verdict moved** — judge scores are informational — but the honest briefing scores worse than the overclaiming one it replaced (0.75 -> 0.0), so it would have been a false red the moment anything gated on it. **Rule now in eval-methodology:** any constraint on how evidence may be read is given to EVERY reader of that evidence — writer, judge, deterministic grader — in the same change, and a rule the reader cannot apply because the fact was stripped from its context is not a rule. Gates: `make check` clean, **2109** unit (2097 before), `make eval-reg` **40/40** exit 0 (canned judge replies, so the offline suite is unchanged), only the `briefing_judge` snapshot hash moves. F-016. |

| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-183 / WP-1.1: `ChaosPlan` — a scenario's fault is a plan, and the plan is put back.** `ChaosPlan(setup, teardown, settle_seconds)` joins `Scenario` additively; legacy `chaos_setup` normalizes to a one-hook plan at load, so every shipped YAML is byte-identical and `make eval-reg` is 41/41. Every hook in either half goes through the existing `ChaosHook` validator against the pinned snapshot. Runner: setup in declared order under the chaos principal (stopping at the first failure), each result recorded evaluator-side with the platform's own refusal NAME, settle, preconditions, run, grade, **teardown on every exit** — clean return, agent crash, unmet precondition, failed seed. Two new failure semantics and two new exit codes (ADR 0037, contract now 0–10): a **setup** failure produces NO `GradeReport` at all (`RunReport.ungraded`, exit 9) because a world that was never built says nothing about the agent; a **teardown** failure leaves the grade standing and latches `evals/.chaos-teardown-block.json`, which refuses every later `--live` run with exit 10 until `make eval-reset PURGE_IDEMPOTENCY=1` + `--clear-chaos-block`. **The thing to not rediscover:** read `Scenario.chaos` / `Scenario.seeds_chaos`, never `chaos_setup` — a plan-declaring scenario leaves the legacy field `None`, so the ADR 0018 smoke refusal, the ADR 0020 exit-7 gate and the `chaos:invoke` guard would each have stopped counting a whole class of scenario with nothing failing. `evals/dossier.py`, `evals/inventory.py` and `evals/fixture_drift.py` still read the legacy field and are the open follow-up (no shipped scenario declares a plan yet, so nothing is wrong today). |
| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-184 / WP-1.2: multi-fault preconditions — VERIFIED, not rebuilt.** The packet asked for behaviour the repo already had, so this PR is the proof and nothing else. `expected_precondition` has been a tuple since it shipped; `_assert_preconditions` already loops every probe, polls each one on its OWN `attempts`/`delay_seconds`, names the failing probe's tool, writes a per-probe `PRECONDITION` trace record, and already splits not-met from unverifiable per probe. What was missing was the acceptance test for a world with more than one fault in it — every precondition test drove a single probe. `TestTwoFaultPreconditions` (6 tests) builds a synthetic two-fault scenario (a two-hook `ChaosPlan`: dead consumer + poisoned cache key, one read-only probe each) and pins, in one shared ordering list across the fake platform and the fake model: both faults seeded then both proven, and only then the first model call; an unmet FIRST premise names only itself and never reaches the second probe; an unmet SECOND premise is reached and named (the half a single-probe suite cannot see); zero LLM calls either way, asserted on the fakes' own call counts; per-probe polling budgets (`[15.0, 15.0, 5.0]`, which a shared budget cannot produce); and the not-met/unverifiable split holding for the second probe. **Do not rediscover:** a canned scenario YAML cannot prove any of this — a canned run ignores preconditions entirely by design — which is why the fixture is synthetic and the suite stays at 41. Zero production lines changed; five of the six tests go red against a one-probe `_assert_preconditions`. Gates: `make check` clean, **2364** unit (2358 before), 31 integration, `make eval-reg` **41/41 exit 0**. |

| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-182 / WP-0.4 stage 2: the Phase 0 baseline, committed and stamped.** `python -m evals.baseline_report --write` persists the assembled baseline as two versioned artifacts under `evals/reports/` — `baseline_report.<stamp>.<invocation>.json` (the record) and `.md` (the same document for a human) — both registered in `evals/artifacts.py` KINDS and resolving through `artifacts.newest`. The stem is deliberately not `baseline`: `evals/reports/baseline.json` is the machine regression baseline `make baseline` blesses, and a shared stem would make this family adopt that file as its own oldest version. **The stamp comes from the run, not from the machine that assembled it.** `stamp_of` collapses the offline run's 41 per-scenario `RunProvenance` records to the one they agree on and refuses if they disagree; `recorded_at`, `scenario` and `budget` differ row to row by design and are excluded from the agreement check, so the report is dated by the run's own `generated_at` and the seeded/used budgets stay per scenario. Every remaining field must be present **and answered** — `"unknown"` is honest in a run archive (ADR 0013) but refused in a baseline, which is divergence D3's un-attributable artifact. Today's `.env` is gone from the document entirely: it described the assembling machine, not the run. Only the offline leg carries a stamp and the report says so rather than borrowing one for the nine live archives, which predate cmd #223. The document is a pure function of committed files — no clock, no environment — and `test_the_committed_baseline_regenerates_byte_for_byte` proves it by re-running the assembler and comparing both halves byte for byte; the offline leg `2408b07ef532` (41/41, development role, `baseline` strategy, commander `ac28475`, platform `sha256:683949…`) is committed alongside so every input to that regeneration is in the repository. The report names the eight green live remediation archives and `cde5a14485c3` literally, explains why that is eight and not STATE.md's nine PASS rows (`e8404306138c` is scenario 2's superseded pre-re-derivation pass), excludes the three never-run-live scenarios with a reason, records `cde5a14485c3`'s missing-group red as the real F-005 finding, and states in its own text that this packet does **not** run `make baseline` and why. **No live or paid run:** zero LLM invocations; nothing under `evals/` was deleted, truncated or overwritten. One line added to `audit-ws/evidence/sync.sh` so the Markdown half mirrors with the JSON. Gates: `make check` clean, **2488** unit, 31 integration, `make eval-reg` **41/41 exit 0**. |
| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-244: re-pin to platform v0.6.4, and give the SLO evaluator its job back.** All three compose services (the `migrate` one-shot included — it runs `alembic upgrade head` off the same image) move to the v0.6.4 INDEX digest `sha256:75eab293ec26…`, confirmed with `docker buildx imagetools inspect` rather than read off a child manifest. **The tool surface did not move:** `make snapshot` against the running v0.6.4 stack reproduces `contracts/platform-tools.snapshot.json` byte for byte (30 tools, md5 `e04d1d46510b56d93e35ee1c76d751fe`, no git diff), so nothing was blessed and there is no delta table — the first re-pin since v0.6.0 with an empty one. **The substance of the packet is the other half:** both `SLO_EVALUATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS: "0"` overrides are gone, so the evaluator runs at its default interval again. That "0" was a stopgap from PR #180 — the evaluator read the seeded fixtures as real traffic (4 of 7 seeded jobs dead-lettered is a fast burn by construction) and raised a critical alert within five minutes of every boot, which `make eval-reset` could not clear because resetting the fixtures re-armed it and its dedup key is hour-bucketed. plat #201 closes both halves (evaluator skips rows carrying the seeded-fixture payload markers, WO-R2-132; reset resolves every active alert outside the five seeded ones, WO-R2-131), and **that fix is inert while the override keeps the loop off** — which is why the bump and the lift had to be one PR. **Proof is on the live world, not in a test:** stack up on v0.6.4, migration `f1c7b93a4d26` (`jobs.max_retries` → `max_attempts`) applied, `SLO_EVALUATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS` unset in both containers, world audit **PASS** at boot and again 25 minutes later with **active alerts still 3** — the two SLO alerts the world is seeded with, and no third. Before v0.6.4 that same 25 minutes produced a fast-burn alert. **Do not rediscover:** the override is not a knob to reach for again; if a fast-burn alert reappears on a seeded world that is a platform regression to report, and the compose comment says so. **Also swept:** the three places that still described the "0" as the current mitigation (Makefile `eval-reset` note, runbook "What `make eval-reset` does NOT clear", the env-var allowlist comment) plus a closing note on the 2026-09 lesson that produced it — a guard comment that was true when written is the hardest kind of stale, and this repo has been bitten by exactly that once already (WO-R2-161). Gates: `make check` clean, **2364** unit, `make test-contract` PASS against the live v0.6.4 stack, `make eval-reg` **41/41 exit 0** (its provenance line stamps `platform sha256:75eab293ec26`). Stack left UP on v0.6.4 at baseline. |
| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-185 / WP-1.3: hidden ground truth, and the wall around it.** `GroundTruth` (`incident_count`, `root_causes` as `HypothesisCategory` values, `affected_components`, `causal_chain`) and `DiscriminatingProbe` (tool + compiled argument-pattern regexes, read tools only) join `Scenario` as two optional, evaluator-only fields. Both are additive: no scenario YAML changed, `make eval-reg` is 41/41 exit 0, and a scenario without a `ground_truth` is simply not root-cause-graded (`Scenario.root_cause_graded` is the coverage predicate WP-2.2 reports on — it is honestly 0/41 today). No action fields on `GroundTruth`, pinned by a test: admissible and forbidden actions stay on `ScenarioExpectation`, cross-checked against `FIX_MAP` (divergence C5, plan 01 § 5). **The thing to not rediscover:** the packet is the wall, not the fields. The agent's whole input is now an allow-list **projection** — `Scenario.agent_visible()` → `AgentVisibleScenario` (frozen, `extra="forbid"`, carrying exactly the alert, the tool-call cap and the canned tool responses) — and `evals/runner.py` builds the run from that object and nothing else. A field added to `Scenario` tomorrow cannot leak by somebody forgetting to exclude it, because there is no exclusion list; `AGENT_VISIBLE_FIELDS` / `EVALUATOR_ONLY_FIELDS` partition every field and an unclassified one raises **at import**. `tests/unit/test_ground_truth_never_leaks.py` renders all four agent-visible contexts (investigation planner, remediation planner, verification judge, briefing writer) for all 41 scenarios twice — as shipped, and with a maximal ground truth attached — and requires the two to be **byte-identical**, which is collision-proof where a substring hunt for labels is not (`poison_message` is also a chaos tool name). Parameterised over the corpus, with its own red-before that breaks the projection the way an exclusion-list design would break it. Gates: `make check` clean, **2427** unit (2358 before), 31 integration, `make eval-reg` 41/41 exit 0. |
| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-186 / WP-1.4: family, difficulty, split and template metadata, with the split enforced at load.** `Scenario` gains `template_id` (defaults to `name`), `seed` (0), `family`, `difficulty` and `benchmark_split` (`dev`) — all five evaluator-only under ADR 0038's partition, because `difficulty` in a prompt narrows the agent's search for free. `family` is a closed enum of what the corpus honestly is (11 members); `difficulty` is plan 03 § 3's closed nine, verbatim, so widening it is a plan change. All 41 YAMLs now declare a family and a difficulty (two lines each, no expectation touched); `evals/benchmark_inventory.json` reads the real values and flips `provisional` to `false`, keeping WO-R3-179's substring rule only as the fallback for an unclassified scenario. **The thing to not rediscover:** splits are by TEMPLATE, never by instance, and the enforcement is a LOAD error — `load_scenarios` refuses a `template_id` appearing under two splits, naming both scenarios, both files and both splits, because the collision is a property of the directory and is invisible in either file on its own. An instance-level holdout leaves siblings of a held-out template in `dev`, a later SFT stage trains on them, and the holdout number then measures memorisation while still being labelled a holdout (plan 06 D7). **Nothing is in `holdout`** — that is a standing promise, so it is the user's call, pinned by `TestNothingIsHeldOutWithoutADecision`. `family`/`difficulty` are optional on the MODEL and mandatory in the CORPUS: a parameterised sweep over `evals/scenarios/` fails a new scenario by name until it is classified, which a required field could not do without forcing a family onto every inline test fixture. The promotion is reconciled rather than asserted — 30 of 41 took WO-R3-179's value unchanged, the other 11 are a table of (rule's guess, chosen value, reason) that is itself a test. `ScenarioOutcome` carries the five keys so a report says what a scenario was *when it ran* (joining back to the YAML re-labels history on every reclassification) and WP-2.5 groups with no schema change; they default to `None` = "predates the record", ADR 0013's precedent. ADR 0039. Gates: `make check` clean, **2801** unit (2427 before), 31 integration, `make eval-reg` 41/41 exit 0. |
| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-245 / WP-1.1 follow-ups: every direct reader of `chaos_setup` migrated, the runbook's two new exit codes, and `make eval-reset` finishing its own job.** `evals/dossier.py`, `evals/inventory.py` and `evals/fixture_drift.py` were the three tools still reading the legacy field, so a plan-declaring scenario would have been reported as seeding nothing by all three — and the dossier would have gone further and *seeded* nothing, then linted an un-faulted world as if it were the scenario's own. All three now read `Scenario.chaos` / `Scenario.seeds_chaos`. The dossier's seeding is extracted as `dossier.seed_chaos`, which fires every setup hook in declared order, stops at the first refusal (naming its position), waits the plan's `settle_seconds` before anything reads, carries the §5.1 sanctioned-row exemption off each hook's own reply, and deliberately does NOT fire a declared teardown — `make eval-reset` is this tool's restore and § 2 of the document now says so. The inventory column `chaos_hook: str | null` becomes `chaos_hooks: list[str]`, because one string cannot describe an ordered plan; `evals/benchmark_inventory.json` regenerated (41 rows, mechanical). **Runbook:** exit rows **9** (setup failed, nothing graded — a statement about the environment, not the agent) and **10** (teardown failed, live runs blocked) with a section each, pinned by a new test that compares the runbook's table to ADR 0037's whole-contract table rather than to a hand-list. **Makefile:** `eval-reset`'s last line is `--clear-chaos-block`, so the command that restores the world is the one that records it — LAST on purpose, since make abandons a recipe at the first failing line and a reset that failed must leave the latch standing (both directions tested). ADR 0037 is untouched: it named this as the expected follow-up and said it changes no decision. **No live or paid run** — every test is unit or canned, the demo stack was not started and no chaos hook was fired. Gates: `make check` clean, **2876** unit (2856 before, +20), 31 integration, `make eval-reg` **41/41 exit 0**. |

The accepted ADR body is unchanged; its appended note records that a failed reset preserves the latch and the standalone clear remains for a torn-down stack. Documentation only. |
| 2026-09-15 | — | **WO-R3-249 / owner decision O-2: the regression baseline is the corpus we actually have, and ADR 0011 is closed.** `make baseline` on `688c00a`, offline and canned under the development model role — **no live or paid run**, zero LLM invocations, nothing under `evals/` deleted, truncated or overwritten. `evals/reports/baseline.json` moves from the pre-campaign **37**-scenario report of 2026-07-31 (cmd #46) to the **41**-scenario report of the current corpus; the superseded baseline is retained in git history, never deleted (invariant 9). **What the gate stops saying:** the standing `new scenarios (4)` notice (`consumer_lag_null_unknown_state`, `dlq_mislabeled_replay_safe`, `dlq_poison_unclassified`, `no_fault_healthy_cache`) is gone, and so is `PROVENANCE: baseline predates provenance stamping (degraded_count unknown)` — the blessed baseline is stamped on all 41 rows with one agreed `RunProvenance` (`claude-sonnet-4-6`, development role, commander `688c00a4c1f2`, platform `sha256:75eab293ec26`, `canned`, `degraded_count` 34), so `make eval-reg` now prints exactly `no changes vs baseline` and exits 0. **What the diff is, dimension by dimension:** 0 dropped scenarios, 0 dropped dimensions, 0 vacated assertions, 0 pass/fail flips; the same five dimension names, graded 185 → **205** times (41 × 5). Two terminal states moved — `dlq_human_required_escalates` and `dlq_mixed_partial`, both `resolved` → `escalated`, which is [ADR 0026](../docs/ADR/0026-a-stabilizer-is-not-a-resolution.md)'s rule that a verified stabilizer is not a resolution, with each scenario's own expectation having moved with it (OUTCOME passes on the new state). Six tool-call counts rose (five 3 → 4, `saga_stuck` 1 → 4): the read-the-row-before-you-replay-it listing from [ADR 0027](../docs/ADR/0027-read-the-row-before-you-replay-it.md)/[0028](../docs/ADR/0028-read-the-category-before-you-replay-it.md). The grader *detail* strings moved on 37 SAFETY, 28 EVIDENCE, 15 BUDGET, 10 ACTION and 2 OUTCOME rows, which is the campaign's grading work finally reaching the baseline: EVIDENCE from substring signals to structured `expected_evidence_fields` (#117), SAFETY from `no forbidden replay ids set` to real negative assertions, ACTION to equivalent-set wording, BUDGET to the caps recalibrated in debt row 4 (#102). `judge_mean_overall` 0.8574 → 0.8591. **ADR 0011 is closed** under owner decision O-3: the Status line now reads `accepted; sunset fired 2026-08-30, closed by the bless of 2026-09-15`, and an appended § Closure records the sunset date, points at the ledger walk in `docs/eval-debt.md` § "Restart walk (2026-09-15)" (cmd #227) as the walk the sunset required before a bless, names this bless as the bless, and restores invariant 8 — `make eval-reg` gates every behaviour-surface PR again and the eval-debt ledger stops being extended. Nothing above the appended section was edited except the Status line. **Do not rediscover — two things.** (1) This baseline is a **development-role** run: `closing: false` in its own provenance, which is the correct input for a regression gate and is *not* a reportable number. A phase-closing figure still needs a benchmark-role run, and blessing one would be a separate deliberate act. (2) The bless is quiet today only because `DEVELOPMENT_MODEL` and `BENCHMARK_MODEL` both fall back to `_DEFAULT_MODEL_ID` (`claude-sonnet-4-6`), so `cross_model_refusal` sees one model. Point `BENCHMARK_MODEL` at a different id and `make eval-reg` under `MODEL_ROLE=benchmark` will **refuse the comparison outright (exit 2)** against this baseline — that is the gate working as designed (a delta across two models is a model change and a behaviour change added together), and the fix is a deliberate re-bless on the new model, not a flag. Two tests that pinned the old baseline's shape as a literal were re-pointed at the new one, each keeping the invariant it states (`tests/unit/test_runner.py::TestBaselineBackwardCompat`, `tests/unit/test_provenance.py::TestArchivedReportsStillParse`). Gates: `make check` clean, **2880** unit, `make eval-reg` **41/41 exit 0**. |
