| 2026-09-16 | — | **WO-R3-187: the two-token split, and the re-pin to platform v0.6.5.** The agent's `PLATFORM_TOKEN` loses `chaos:invoke`; every seed/reset/chaos path moves to a new `PLATFORM_CHAOS_TOKEN` (`incident-commander-chaos`, the evaluator's own principal, deliberately without `actions:execute`). This is half of a mechanism: v0.6.5 hides `chaos.%` audit and trace rows from principals that cannot fire chaos, and that filter was **inert** here because the runner seeded each fault under the agent's own token — `list_audit_events` answered "who broke this?" with the hook name and its arguments, seconds before the alert the agent was investigating (divergence G3, owner decision O-4). `bootstrap_agent_token.py` now mints three accounts, strips `chaos:invoke` from an existing agent account, and refuses `--scope chaos:invoke`. `assert_chaos_blind_principal` is the new negative guard; `assert_chaos_capable_principal` moved to the evaluator's client; both share `_assert_scope_absent`, which distinguishes an arguments refusal (scope IS carried) from never reaching the handler (proves nothing) — a `-32601` from a chaos-disabled stack used to read as a wrong credential. Found on the way: CI's `test-idempotency` would have **silently skipped** under the agent principal, green while asserting nothing about the dedup guarantee ADR 0008 rests on (ci.yml now exports a third token; `chaos_client` fixture). Snapshot **byte-identical** (30 tools) — `tools/list` is not scope-filtered, ADR 0016 defers that. Verified live on v0.6.5: `make eval-reg` 41/41 no changes vs baseline, world audit PASS 15/15, and a zero-LLM four-way guard proof (agent token refused `-32002` on the chaos probe, evaluator's refused `-32602` on arguments). |
| 2026-09-16 | — | **WO-R3-252:** correct `_print_provenance` docstring for the stamped baseline, warn-only degraded counts, and separate hard cross-model refusal. No behavior change. |
| 2026-09-16 | — | **WO-R2-177:** generate the methodology remediation table’s current claims from validated scenarios and lint for drift. Preserve historical failures, and correct the stale unclassified-row fence and poison replay-count narrative. **Row membership is derived, not listed:** a scenario is in the table when it requires an action *or* forbids the action tools, because forbidding them is the claim that the correct action count is zero. The first cut listed the rows by hand and kept `consumer_lag_high` as the one no-action example, which left `consumer_lag_healthy_zero` and `no_fault_healthy_cache` — two do-nothing controls with the full seven-tool forbidden set — outside both the table and the lint. That is the same drift channel the table was built to close, on the exact claim the suite has graded wrong twice (WO-R2-140, WO-R2-160). |
| 2026-09-17 | — | **WO-R2-174: the bounded output repair now covers both judges.** cmd #216 gave the decoder plus one re-ask to the three planner call sites; the verification judge (`remediation.make_llm_verify`) and the eval briefing judge (`graders/llm_judge.py`) got the decoder only. Both now use the same `call_with_output_repair` wrapper at the same cap of 1 — no second implementation, no per-site budget. The verify judge is the one that mattered: it runs **after** a Tier-1 action has executed, so a judgment the schema rejected escalated the run and told a human "verification incomplete" about a fix that had landed. Its accrual moved from `accrue_llm_usage` to `accrue_structured_call` so both legs of a repaired judgment reach the ledger (ADR 0015). The eval judge's rule is the opposite one: a malformed score must never become a number, and it does not — a second failure raises `OutputRepairExhausted`, an `LLMError`, so the runner keeps `judge_score` `None` and records `judge_error`; the repair just buys one try before the column is given up. Scope, cap and prompts unchanged; a transport error is still not re-asked. Canned runs are untouched (`CannedLLMClient` raises a plain `LLMError` when its script runs out, which is not repairable), so `make eval-reg` stays 41/41 no changes vs baseline. ADR 0035 amended, not rewritten. |
| 2026-09-17 | — | **WO-R3-251 + WO-R3-254 commander halves: one re-pin to platform v0.6.7.** v0.6.6 took the hook's name out of the poison row's error text; v0.6.7 gave `get_consumer_lag` the time of its reading — `measured_at`, `age_seconds`, and `recent_samples` (the last five measurements, newest first), plus a description paragraph saying a trend is read from ONE call and that a re-read inside the ~60s refresh window returns the same number. That is the root fix for red live run `42000dfda188`, which re-read lag three times in 25s, saw 29 → 29 → 29, and read a metric that had not been re-measured as a metric that was not moving. **Snapshot delta: exactly one tool** — `get_consumer_lag` gains three OPTIONAL properties and a `LagSample` `$defs`, keeps its `required` list and its `inputSchema`, description 2222 → 3401 chars; the other 29 tools are byte-identical. The registry's output model mirrors the new shape (`test_registry_matches_snapshot` compares titles strictly, so the nested model must be named `LagSample`). Fourteen canned lag responses across thirteen scenarios re-recorded: static and unrecognized groups null/null/[], the four worker-dispatcher readings carrying a window whose newest entry IS the reading and whose trend is the scenario's story. Seven poison-text places moved, including the precondition the dossier reads back. **The drift lesson:** the new fields are the reading's freshness metadata the `get_consumer_lag` volatile note reserved a slot for in v0.6.0, and pinning them made the ledger FLAP rather than disagree — on the re-record run a canned `age_seconds: 12` happened to equal a live 12 and the drift key vanished for one fixture. `recent_samples` is worse than volatile: its EMPTINESS is a function of how long the stack has been up (the metrics loop sleeps 60s before its first measurement, and `make eval-reset` clears the window), so the same fixture lands under `no_live_rows` one run and `not_live_reachable` the next — the `_differs_in_type` null-vs-zero lesson, applied to list emptiness. All of it is declared volatile, a volatile list is now exempt from the `no_live_rows` finding, and only **three** ledger entries are added, all `tool_output_schema_mismatch`'s deliberate omission. Verified on the live v0.6.7 stack: `make eval-reg` 41/41 no changes vs baseline, world audit PASS 15/15, `make fixture-drift` 0 new / 0 stale / 0 defects, and two zero-LLM dossiers — the poison one shows no `chaos` anywhere in the preconditions or in any agent-side read, the lag one captures the new fields live. |
| 2026-09-17 | — | **Comment pass over the eval harness.** Short module-, class- and function-level docstrings added where a public piece of `evals/` or `scripts/` had none: the grader's five scoring functions and its result models, the regression gate's entry point, the audit-window scan's merge and inconclusive message, the chaos client's `call`, the fixture drift/shape/probe walkers and their report rows, the world audit's baseline line and trace-freshness check, the dossier's renderers, and every script's `main`. **Comments and docstrings only — zero code changes**, proved by an AST comparison with docstrings stripped. Files already carrying an explanation were left exactly as they were; `evals/tracing.py`, `evals/runner.py`, `evals/artifacts.py` and `scripts/format_traces.py` were left for a follow-up pass because other work was open on them. `make check` clean, 2938 unit tests green. |
| 2026-09-17 | — | **Comment pass A: docstrings on the agent's own code.** Eighteen short docstrings (mostly one line) added where a function had none and its purpose was not stated nearby: the three prompt-context builders (`investigation._format_planner_context`, `remediation._format_plan_context`, `briefing_enrichment._format_context`), the two `_escalate` helpers, `briefing._render_alert_summary`, `remediation._tool_context_block` / `_summarize_output`, `triage._severity`, the `/alerts` route and its background-run closure, `LLMClient.call` / `_parse`, the MCP client's `list_tools` / `call_tool` / `_call`, and `PostgresCheckpointer.load`. Comments and docstrings only — no code changed, AST-compared against `origin/main` with docstrings stripped. **`tools/registry.py`'s 45 schema models were deliberately left alone:** a Pydantic class docstring becomes a `description` in `model_json_schema()`, and those input schemas are rendered verbatim into the investigation and remediation planner prompts, so a docstring there would change what the live agent reads. Most of this tree already carries long explanatory comments and was skipped as already clear; `config.py`, `agent/factory.py` and `agent/strategies/` were left for a follow-up pass because other work was open on them. |

The reason is measurement, not generosity: a best-of-8 strategy spends roughly eight times the tokens and dollars of the control group on the same incident, and metered against the fleet ceilings it would exhaust a budget mid-investigation and escalate — a report that then reads as the STRATEGY failing rather than as the budget declining to fund it (plan 02 § 8). The multipliers are **ratios**, because the absolute numbers are not in this repo: the paid protocol's budgets (1.00 USD / 25 calls / 200000 tokens / 600 s) live only in the operator's un-committed `.env` (divergence D7), so a test can assert the multiplier reached the seed and must never assert an absolute. **Applied exactly once**, where `agent/factory.py::start_run` seeds the ledger — `Settings.seeded_max_tokens` / `seeded_max_usd` are the scaled ceilings and `start_run` is their only consumer, pinned by a source scan (`tests/unit/test_budgets.py::TestNoOtherCallSiteScalesABudget`), because a second scaling site would fail no behavioural test and every cost number in every report would quietly mean two things. **Never multiplied: tool calls and wall seconds.** Probing the world is what the strategies compete on, and that same number is the scenario's grading cap ([ADR 0019](../docs/ADR/0019-scenario-cap-is-the-runtime-ceiling.md)) — scaling it would fund one strategy's extra probes *and* move the bar it is graded against. No wall multiplier is declared at all; longer runs come from `BUDGET_MAX_SECONDS`, for the whole invocation and in plain sight. **The trap this packet exists to not fall into:** `start_run` has ignored a `max_tool_calls` of `0` since ADR 0019 because `BudgetLedger.is_exhausted` is `used >= max`, so a zero ledger is born exhausted and the run escalates before TRIAGE having investigated nothing — which reads exactly like a ceiling working as designed. Tokens and dollars had no such guard and a multiplier is a new way to reach zero, directly or by rounding (0.001 of a 100-token budget is no tokens). Both are refused at `Settings` construction, naming the dimension and both variables that produced it, never clamped. Baseline's multipliers are 1, and the seeded baseline ledger is asserted equal to the pre-packet one field for field. BUDGET stays beside correctness: it grades the tool-call cap, which no multiplier touches, so a correct run over the *baseline* budget is a frontier point whose four meters travel in its provenance row, not a failure. Scoring is set-shaped — exact set is the pass condition, precision/recall/F1 are measured partial credit that never turns a wrong answer green — so a strategy that emits several diagnoses needs no change here. `NO_FAULT` needs no branch: the control declares `[no_fault]`, the schema refuses to pair it with a fault, and the level-0 case is the ordinary exact match. **Coverage is 0 of 41 and the summary says so in those words** (`root cause: not measured — 0 of 41 scenario(s) declare a ground truth`), which is a different statement from 0%; back-filling a guessed label onto 41 scenarios written without one would manufacture the number the dimension exists to measure. A scenario with no ground truth grades vacuously in the shape `is_vacuous_detail` matches, so the gate keeps its vacated-assertion check over the dimension, and a NEW dimension is invisible to `dropped_dimensions` (`baseline − latest`) — asserted against the committed 41-scenario baseline, not a synthetic pair. | 2026-09-17 | — | **WO-R3-257: `evals/reports/` becomes navigable, and a run stops writing 39 files.** Owner request: "the human folder under reports was not organized, that whole reports folder should be better organized". The folder held 100 flat `report.<ts>.<id>.json` beside `baseline.json`, a flat `dossiers/`, and a `human/` of **765 files for 56 distinct runs** — 93% repeats, all of them permanent, because invariant 9 forbids deleting any. **The root cause was the renderer, not the folder:** `scripts/format_traces.py` is chained after the runner in `make eval-live`/`eval-smoke` and re-rendered EVERY trace file it could find, so a one-scenario live run wrote one report of the run that happened and 38 fresh copies of trajectories nobody had touched. It is now incremental — it renders a scenario whose newest traced attempt no existing report covers, which is both "the run that just ran" and "a scenario whose last render was lost", and skips the rest with a count; `--force` re-renders deliberately. Coverage is read back from the `Invocation:` header the renderer itself writes, because the id in a report's FILENAME names the render session, not the traced run — the filename cannot answer the question and the file can. **The layout is data in `evals/artifacts.py` and nowhere else:** each `ArtifactKind` gained a `folder` and a `grouping`, so `runs/<YYYY-MM>/`, `baseline/`, `phase-close/`, `dossiers/<scenario>/` and `human/<scenario>/` are derived by the same module that names the files and resolves the newest one — the migration script computes every destination by calling it, so the mover and the resolver cannot disagree. `baseline.json` and `latest.json` do not move (the gate and `evals.yml`'s filter name the first by literal path). **Reads cover the old flat place permanently**, not just during the transition: that is where the legacy un-versioned names live, and it is what keeps the window between this merging and the migration running from being a window where the gate sees an empty reports folder. **`scripts/migrate_reports_layout.py` is the only thing in the repo that moves an eval artifact** — `--dry-run` prints and files the manifest, `--root` points it at the hub mirror as well, it sha256's every file before and after and refuses to claim success unless the count and the digest multiset are unchanged, it refuses a destination that exists rather than overwriting (which is also what makes the second run a no-op), it unlocks only what it must and puts every mode and `uchg` flag back including on directories it creates, and it `git mv`s tracked files so history follows. Nothing is deleted: earlier renders of a run that has a newer one move to `human/_superseded/<scenario>/`, which `artifacts.versions()` still searches — a superseded render can never be `newest()` by construction, so nothing hides. It refuses any folder not named `reports`, so it cannot be aimed at `evals/runs/`. **Rehearsed against copies, never the real tree:** the checkout's 918 files and the hub mirror's locked 918 both migrated with every sha256 unchanged and relocked as found. Gates: `make check` clean, **3102** unit + 31 integration, `make eval-reg` 41/41 "no changes vs baseline" — the gate resolving its report from the new `runs/2026-09/` folder. `evals/artifacts.py` added to `evals.yml`'s path filter: it decides which report the gate grades, and a change to it could move a verdict with no prompt or grader touched. |

## Things a future session should not have to rediscover

Promoted out of the archives because they cost real time or money the first time.

- **`gaps.json` records have been read backwards.** At least one — `G1-declared-but-uncon-02` —
  says "chaos_setup **blocks** for 4 of 7 hooks", a *noun* meaning the YAML blocks that declare
  them. Three documents read it as a verb and an entire build item was aimed at a defect that
  did not exist. **Verify a gap against the code before building against it.**
- **Nothing was seeding the demo stack, for the whole project.** `SEED_EVAL_FIXTURES` was set on
  the one service that never runs the startup hook that reads it. It survived because the only CI
  job that boots the stack diffs `tools/list`, which needs no rows — an empty database looked
  exactly like a full one. Fixed, but it means live-run evidence predating the fix ran against an
  environment nobody had described.
- **A foreground eval run gets killed by the 10-minute command timeout.** It happened once and
  wasted the spend. Long runs go in the background, always.
- **An investigation agent bypassed a safety prompt** with `yes |` and `--no-confirm`, trimmed a
  shared Kafka topic, and crash-looped three consumer groups into a full stack rebuild. Read-only
  means read-only; an agent routing around a denial is a thing that happens.
- **`git stash` is shared across worktrees.** `refs/stash` is one stack per repository, so a stash
  taken in one worktree is visible and poppable from another. Three obsolete entries are still
  sitting in the commander checkout.
- ~~**Every `make chaos-*` target is broken at import**~~ — **fixed 2026-08-16.** `python
  scripts/x.py` put `scripts/` on `sys.path[0]` instead of the repo root, so `import evals`
  failed. The recipes now set `PYTHONPATH=.`. They still need `.env` sourced by hand, which is
  deliberate: make does not `-include .env` because that is exactly what silently overrode the
  token in PR #62.
- **A default that only CI exercises is a default nobody tests.** `make bootstrap-token` named a
  container from the *platform's* dev compose, so the documented `make demo && make
  bootstrap-token` pair could never work — CI passed `--postgres-container` explicitly and never
  saw it. Found by running the documented path, not by reading it.

- **Subagents stall at "waiting for CI".** Their background watchers die when the agent stops, so a
  PR sits green and unmerged forever. Shepherd them: on each completion notification check the PR
  yourself — green+CLEAN, merge it directly (cheaper than resuming); red, resume the agent with a
  `gh run view <id> --log-failed` pointer. Tell agents to poll `gh pr checks` themselves.
- **ADR 0021's `uchg` archive locking blocks `git worktree remove`.** Needs `chflags -R nouchg` plus
  `chmod -R u+w` first. Bites anyone who ran `make eval-reg` inside a worktree.
- **The repo `.venv` editable install pins to the MAIN checkout's `backend/`,** so a repo-root
  `pytest` from any worktree imports master's code and never sees your changes — it surfaced as 73
  phantom failures. Run with `PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/backend`. (platform repo)
- **`git branch --no-merged` and patch-id comparison BOTH lie under squash-merge.** Squashing rewrites
  commits, so merged branches look unmerged forever and N-commits-to-1 defeats patch-id. Check branch
  names against the merged-PR record, then compare file CONTENT. Two separate sessions concluded
  "unmerged work exists" from these; both were wrong.
- **`git add -A` in the main checkout swept 342 files into a 6-line PR.** PR #178 changed six lines
  of the runbook and also committed three untracked run archives (~42k lines) that happened to be
  sitting in `evals/runs/`. That directory is deliberately NOT gitignored (invariant 9), which is
  exactly what makes `-A` dangerous there rather than merely noisy. **Add explicit paths, never
  `-A`.** Run archives are committed deliberately, on their own branch, with their own message —
  never as a side effect. Doing docs work in a worktree prevents it structurally.
- **Protocol check beats machinery check.** Before a paid run, verifying that the machinery works
  is the *second* question; the first is whether you are running the runbook's exact command. The
  2026-08-30 read-only stage was executed as a hand-rolled `ONLY=` list under the write token
  instead of `make eval-smoke` — every property that made the stage read-only came from the target,
  so all of them were absent, agents remediated during it, and ~$2 of results were discarded. A
  substring in that list also smuggled a mutating scenario past the ADR 0020 gate (the gate counts
  mutators, and one is allowed). **Deviations from a documented paid-run command go to the user
  before the spend, not into a workaround.** The trap had been written down 19 days earlier and
  nobody was routed to it — hence the runbook's pre-run checklist now opens by pointing at the
  gotchas ledger as step 1.

- **Offline eval never loads a prompt, and `FIX_MAP`'s values are never read at runtime.** Canned
  scenarios replay recorded planner output, so the prompt is a live-only surface and 38/38 says
  nothing about it; and the handoff gate reads only `top.category not in FIX_MAP`, so a wrong value
  in that map breaks no test. Both were stale in the same direction for the whole life of PR #173
  (2026-09-07). Before a paid run, read the prompt the live agent will load against the scenario it
  will be graded by — the regression suite structurally cannot do it for you.

- **A guard stack can be complete about the object and silent about the decision.** By 2026-09-07
  five plan guards established that a remediation named the right resource, sourced it from the
  platform, verified it on the same resource, could observe what it changed, and was worth something
  when verified. All five admitted a plan to replay a dead-lettered job on no evidence beyond the
  fact that it had failed. **When adding a guard, ask which of two questions it answers — "is this
  aimed correctly?" or "should this happen at all?" — because the first kind accumulates and looks
  like the second.**
- **`_unsourced_resource_args` is a hallucination check, not a provenance check.** It proves the
  platform emitted a string. An id from the alert payload passes it, which is why "evidence-sourced"
  must never be read as "somebody looked this resource up".
- **A refusal written under a new marker name is invisible to the planner.** `_format_plan_context`
  matched `_PLAN_REFUSED_MARKER` exactly, so a second refusal shape landed inside the 200-character
  evidence truncation — cut mid-sentence, which is the one thing that function's own comment says
  the whole-rendering exists to prevent. Refusal markers are a derived set now
  (`_PLAN_REFUSAL_MARKERS`); a third shape must join it.
- **The fixture-drift check cannot see an error text.** `_UNBOUNDED_TEXT` in
  `evals/fixture_drift.py` exempts `items.error_message`, `items.triage.summary` and
  `items.triage.suggested_fix` from comparison, because a free-text field's live domain is unbounded
  and membership in it says nothing. The reasoning is sound and the consequence is not obvious: a
  canned `error_message` can say anything at all and `make fixture-drift` still reports 0 new, 0
  stale. Six fixtures carried a YAML round-trip artifact in that field (`''user_id''` where the
  platform emits `'user_id'`) for the life of the project, and the v0.6.1 texts drifted there
  invisibly. **A green drift report is not evidence the error texts are right.** What does check
  them is `make world-dossier`'s §5 coherence lint (hint vs error family) and reading the row. When
  a release moves error texts, diff the fixtures against a live read by hand — the ratchet will not
  do it for you.

- **The drift ratchet DOES see a new field, and the two exemption lists do not hide it.** (2026-09-08,
  v0.6.2 re-pin.) The complement of the entry above, and the pair is what makes the check readable.
  `_VOLATILE` and `_UNBOUNDED_TEXT` are **leaf-value** exemptions, applied inside `walk`'s leaf branch
  and `walk_leaves`; the key-set diff (`set(live) - set(canned)` → `live_only_field`, and the reverse
  → `canned_only_field`) runs BEFORE them, on every mapping including rows merged out of a list by
  `_merge_rows` (which unions keys across rows, so a field only some rows carry is still compared).
  That is how `DlqEntry`'s new `fenced_at`/`fenced_by` surfaced as drift on 30 canned rows across
  seven scenarios the moment the pin moved. So the two lists are safe to extend for a value that
  cannot be pinned — a clock, a per-boot principal id — without turning the field itself optional:
  **presence stays enforced, only the value stops being asserted.** One more thing worth knowing when
  a release adds a nullable field: a canned `null` against an all-null live domain reports nothing at
  all, because `walk_leaves` strips `None` from both the domain and the canned values before
  comparing. Whether a value LANDED is a scenario claim, never a ratchet claim.

- **A guard's stated reason can expire without the guard changing.** (2026-09-08, v0.6.2 re-pin.)
  `SOURCE_ROW_FOR_ACTION["mark_dlq_permanent"]` is declared inert, and the reason written at the map
  entry is that "the mark stops auto-replay, it does not re-run anything — so acting on an unread row
  cannot cause the harm this guard exists to prevent" (WO-R2-144). Half of that premise died with
  plat #198: a fence now stamps `fenced_at`/`fenced_by` and writes an audit row on **any** row, so
  fencing a row nobody read leaves a permanent attributable mark on it. The conclusion still holds —
  a fence re-runs nothing, which is the harm ADR 0027 is about — so the entry is left alone and the
  ordering is graded by the scenario instead (WO-R2-161). **When a platform release changes what an
  action DOES, re-read the reasons every inert guard entry gives, not just the entries themselves:**
  a comment that was true when written is the hardest kind of stale.

- **A check that reasons over evidence needs a boundary, and a green test can be sitting on the
  wrong side of it.** (2026-09-08, WO-R2-164.) The alerted-condition check asks "which dead-letter
  rows did this run see before it acted?", and the boundary is the action's own evidence entry —
  `make_remediate` writes one under `plan.action_tool`. Two anti-vacuity controls in the
  stabilizer classes build a VERIFYING state directly, with no such entry, so the boundary search
  fell off the end and the POST-action **verify** page was being read as pre-action evidence. Both
  tests were green, and both would have kept being green while the check answered from the wrong
  reading. The fix is to DECLINE when the boundary is absent, not to fall back to "everything":
  a real run always has it, and a fallback that silently widens the window is worse than an inert
  guard. **The general rule: any evidence-derived check has a before/after boundary, and the test
  to write is the one where the boundary is missing.**

- **An exact claim written against a lying fixture is worse than a vague one.** (2026-09-08,
  v0.6.3 re-pin, WO-R2-166.) cmd #184 replaced `remediate_dlq_backlog_success`'s
  `replayed at_least 1` with `which: sum, equals: 2` and called it exact. It was exact — about a
  world the lab was lying about. The second replay-safe row was a schema-invalid payload that
  `poison_message` had stamped `replay_safe` since the hook shipped, so the tightening did not just
  fail to catch the defect, it *pinned* it: an agent that replayed only the transient row would now
  have graded RED. The vague claim was wrong and looked wrong; the exact claim was wrong and looked
  authoritative, and it survived a review that was explicitly hunting for weak claims. **Rule:** when
  you write a count into a claim, say in the comment WHERE the number comes from and check that
  source against the code that produces the rows — cmd #184's own comment cited the hook's snapshot
  description, which was the lie, and citing it made the number look derived.

- **A pair of any-row assertions is one claim short of the claim you meant.** (2026-09-08.)
  `items[].id equals <chaos row>` and `items[].remediation_hint is_null: true` read together as "the
  injected fault landed unclassified" and are satisfied by two DIFFERENT rows — the chaos row being
  present, and something else being unclassified. `RowSelector` fixed this on the grader side at
  cmd #206 and the precondition side kept the hole for two more releases (WO-R2-163), because the
  gap was recorded in a comment instead of closed. `PreconditionField` now takes the same `where`,
  validated against the grader's own rule so the two cannot drift. **Rule:** whenever two assertions
  about a list appear together and the sentence joining them is "and", ask which row each one is
  about.

- **A refusal code that never reaches the message reads as flakiness.** (2026-09-08, WO-R2-16.)
  Every tool-level refusal the platform raises is JSON-RPC `-32011`; the name is one level down in
  `data.error_code`, and `ChaosClient` was rendering only the code and the prose. So
  `poison_fixture_name_in_use` — "a previous run's row is still here and has drifted" — arrived
  looking like a transport error, and the reflex for a transport error is to re-run, which seeds the
  same refusal again. The codes are ledgered in `evals/chaos_hooks.py` with the sentence that
  matters ("reset the world, do not retry"), and the NAME comes off the wire rather than out of the
  table, so a code shipped tomorrow loses the advice and never the identity.

- **`make test-drift` failed once and passed five times on the same world, and the cause is not
  established.** (2026-09-08, v0.6.3 re-pin.) Immediately after `make fixture-drift-bless` +
  `make fixture-drift` reported 0 new / 0 stale, the pytest sibling reported 7 fixtures outside the
  ledger and 3 ledger entries no longer drifting — the pre-bless numbers and the just-added entries.
  Five consecutive re-runs since, including one immediately after `make eval-reset`, are green, and
  `make fixture-drift` has stayed 0/0. Recorded rather than explained: the two commands fire the same
  29 live calls against a shared stack seconds apart, so a world that moved between them is the
  obvious suspect and nothing proves it. **Rule:** if you see this pair disagree, re-run
  `make fixture-drift` FIRST and compare its numbers — the ledger is only wrong if both agree it is.

- **A known failure mode with a one-field mitigation is a mitigated field, not a mitigated failure
  mode.** (2026-09-08, run `779b19a287a7`, WO-R2-173.) `InvestigationStep.next_action` carried a
  `json.loads` coercion from July 2026 with an honest comment saying Anthropic's tool-use
  "sometimes emits nested oneOf fields as JSON strings" — so the failure was known, seen, and
  patched on the one field where it had been seen. Five other nested fields across four other
  `record_output` models had nothing, and the coercion read as solved for six weeks. It then fired
  on its own field and still lost the run, because it was a strict `json.loads` against a string
  that carried one stray `]`. **Rule:** when a defect is about a CLASS of field, the fix belongs
  where the class is defined — `cls.model_fields` and the annotations, not a list beside the
  schema; and a coercion with no negative tests is a coercion nobody has bounded.

- **A RED is not automatically a measurement of the agent.** (2026-09-08.) If a run ended because
  the harness could not decode the agent's own `record_output` payload, nothing about its judgement
  was measured — the answer is in the trace and the escalation describes the harness. Such a run is
  fixed and re-run (with a fresh go), and `report.json` now says so: `failure_class:
  planner_output_invalid`, checked ahead of the `transport` bucket, which two of the three
  output-invalid escalation reasons would otherwise match on the words "LLM" and "invalid".

## Standing rules that outlive any session

- **No paid eval run without explicit permission, in plain words, each time.** A readiness
  confirmation is not authorization.
- **No remediation eval** until the fixture work is done.
- **Eval artifacts are append-only** (invariant 9). Nothing under `evals/{runs,reports,trajectories,briefings}/`
  or `study/` is ever deleted, truncated, or overwritten.
- **`contracts/platform-tools.snapshot.json` is generated.** Never hand-edited.

| 2026-09-17 | — | **WO-R3-260: the briefing writer's LLM call is the agent's own cost, so it is charged to the run ledger — metered, never gating. And `StepRecord.llm_calls[].elapsed_ms` stops being null.** ADR 0015 § 4 kept `enrich_briefing` out of `BudgetLedger` because a ceiling cannot gate a call that happens after the last budget check. That is true, and it answered a different question from the one asked: **a ledger is a meter as well as a ceiling.** Every run buys exactly one briefing-writer call, so every cost-per-run figure the harness produced undercounted the agent by one call, always in the same direction — a rounding error in a baseline-only world and a bias in the strategy comparisons Phases 5 and 6 exist to draw, where the arm with fewer planner steps carries the larger relative error. WP-2.3 recorded the gap (`charged_to_ledger: false`) and recording a gap is not closing one. The claim that its cost was "visible in the trace files" was also weaker than it read: a trace file exists only when `EVAL_TRACE_DIR` is set, which the whole canned suite does not set, so on every offline run that call's cost was visible nowhere at all. **`enrich_briefing` now takes the ledger and returns the one it charged**, the same shape `_plan_next_step` uses, so a call site that forgets the charge is a type error rather than a silent under-report — and mypy found all eleven of them. The evaluator's `briefing_judge` stays outside: the line `charged_to_ledger` draws is **whose money a call is**, not when it was made, which is a narrower and more defensible rule than "after the terminal state" (that one put the agent's own prose on the evaluator's side). **Never gating is structural, not a promise.** The runner keeps the charged ledger in a local and never writes it back onto the terminal `RunState`: `grade(final, …)` still reads the ledger the agent finished with, so the BUDGET dimension cannot be decided by a charge made after the agent was done, and a checkpoint is not mutated after the run ended (a resumed run would read a budget it never spent). The provenance and accounting rows carry the charged ledger, and the difference between the two numbers is exactly the metered-but-not-gating spend. A failed enrichment is charged too, through the same `accrue_llm_error` the investigation loop uses. **One defect found on the way and fixed in the same packet:** a crash AFTER enrichment (a grader raising) would have reconciled a split containing the briefing call against the last checkpoint, which predates it, and printed `COST UNRECONCILED` over a run where nothing disagreed — `ScenarioCrash` now carries the ledger it charged, with the checkpoint as the fallback for a crash that never got that far. **The second half: `LLMResult.elapsed_ms`.** WP-2.1 filled every other field on the step record and left this one `None` with an honest comment saying nothing in `llm/client.py` timed a call. Nothing did — the only duration was a per-attempt `duration_seconds` in a trace file the offline suite never writes. The client now times its **logical** call, every retried attempt and every backoff sleep included, because that is the latency the loop waited out; `_plan_next_step` carries it on `PlannerCall` and `baseline` puts it on the record. It sits in the same bucket as the four token counters (the call that PARSED), not with the ledger delta, because a repair's rejected leg is a separate call with its own duration. **`None` is kept meaningful**: a client that does not time itself reports it, so every canned step record still reads `null` rather than a fabricated zero, and there is a test pinning that. `llm/client.py` gained an injectable `clock` for the same reason it already had an injectable `sleep` — a duration measured against the real monotonic clock can only be asserted as `>= 0`, which passes against a broken measurement. **ADR 0015 carries a dated amendment (appended; § 4's text is not rewritten, and a pointer line under it names the amendment), and it answers that ADR's own revisit trigger differently than the trigger guessed: no new accounting surface was needed** — keeping the post-terminal ledger off `RunState` is what the trigger was really protecting, and a returned value does that without a second meter to reconcile against the first. **How the regression gate read the ledger change, since the order asks for it exactly:** `no changes vs baseline`, exit 0. It is not that the gate looked and forgave — `regression.compare` walks six things (regressions, improvements, new and dropped scenarios, dropped dimensions, vacated assertions), all derived from `report.passed` and each dimension's `detail`, and it reads provenance for exactly two purposes: `agent_model` (a hard cross-model refusal) and `degraded_count` (a warn-only print). `provenance.budget` and the whole `accounting` record are carried on every row and compared by nothing. The numbers did not move either: `CannedLLMClient` bills zero, so all 41 rows still read `tokens_used: 0` and `usd_used: 0.000000`, and the only provenance-budget difference against the blessed baseline is `wall_seconds_used` — microseconds of machine noise that differ between any two runs. What DID move is the flag: all 41 rows now carry `briefing_writer charged_to_ledger: true` where main had `false`, and `briefing_judge` is the only `false` left. The committed baseline predates WP-2.3 and carries no `accounting` record at all, so there is nothing on that side to compare against even in principle. Nothing was re-blessed. Gates: `make check` clean, **3163** unit (+15, 14 of them red-before proven — the fifteenth asserts a canned record still reads `null`, which is green before and after by design and is there to keep it that way) + 31 integration (8 live-gated cases skipped, no credentials in the environment); `make eval-reg` 41/41, `root cause: 32/32 correct (100%)`, `no changes vs baseline`, exit 0, no `COST UNRECONCILED`; archives left untracked. No live or paid run, no stack touched. **Divergences reported, not worked around:** the order's `file_ownership` omits three files the change cannot be made without — `agent/strategies/records.py` (`PlannerCall` is the carrier, and `LLMCallRecord.elapsed_ms`'s own docstring said the field is null because nothing times a call), `evals/runner.py` (the metering flag, the accrual, the crash path) and `docs/eval-methodology.md`, whose accounting table stated as fact that the briefing writer is not metered. The fact is PASSED IN to `grade()` (`world_matches_ground_truth`), beside the label it qualifies, for the reason the label itself is passed in: `grade()` stays a pure function of its arguments and must not learn what a platform is, because the offline re-grade reads that fact out of an archive instead. Where the label does not apply the dimension is VACUOUS, not green and not red: detail `not graded: the label describes a world this run did not have — …`, which `is_vacuous_detail` now recognises as a second shape, so the row leaves the accuracy denominator, the regression gate keeps its vacated-assertion check, and the summary prints the two counts separately. **The rule is about the world, never about whether the row would pass** — holding back the reds and keeping the greens would buy the same invalid number back with a friendlier sign. Names differing only in case are deliberately NOT folded together and that is pinned as a test — `name` is operator-facing free text, and the duplicate *rate* WP-5.2 reports measures what the model produced, not what a normaliser could hide. **The stringified payload is not hand-written.** No live candidate-set payload exists (WP-5.2 is what would emit one), so the malformation is taken from the one we have: run `779b19a287a7`'s `next_action`, imported from `test_structured_output.py` so it stays byte-identical to the evidence, with its stray closer read off the string rather than retyped, and the candidate content is that run's own two diagnoses. (2) The CLOCKS are re-based once, at load: `SHIFTED_CLOCK_FIELDS` adds one rigid document-wide offset to every absolute timestamp, `HELD_DURATION_FIELDS` leaves `age_seconds` / `ttl_seconds` / `paused_expires_in_seconds` exactly as recorded, and both halves say the same thing — the replay clock IS the moment of the reading, so `now - measured_at == age_seconds` still holds. Per-call anchoring was rejected because the same DLQ row read through two tools would then carry two `created_at` values, and decrementing the durations was rejected because a recording replayed a day later would hold an expired pause. The two lists are written down per tool with reasons; a test derives the expected coverage from the registry's output models, so a time field shipped tomorrow fails a test instead of replaying a stale timestamp. (3) TIER-1 is refused through `policies.tier_of` over the whole registry (never a name list) and the refusal RAISES a non-`MCPError` — transitions catch `MCPError` and escalate, so an action refused that way would make a run that could not have remediated read as a run that chose to escalate. Defence in depth beside `_execute_probe`'s own tier check, not a replacement for it. The client also refuses to construct without a recording or with an empty one, and cannot fall back to a real client: no `Settings`, no URL, no token, no transport — asserted on the module's syntax tree. 38 new unit tests (3411 unit total), `make eval-reg` 41/41 with `root cause: 32/32` and no changes vs baseline. Red-before proved four ways by breaking the code: raw-keyed lookup (13 red), no re-base (5 red), a healthy empty miss (2 red), a name-list refusal (1 red). NOTE for whoever reads a recorded result: the replay client is built and proved against the four committed recordings with the canned planner; `--mode recorded` with a real model is a PAID run and is deferred (WP-3.3, WO-R3-198). |
| 2026-09-17 | — | **WO-R3-198 (WP-3.3, [ADR 0047](../docs/ADR/0047-a-recorded-run-grades-diagnosis-and-the-plan-and-nothing-else.md)): `--mode recorded --world <id>` and `make world-drift` — the mode every later paired comparison runs in, and the check that keeps its results honest.** A recorded run is a REAL model against a REPLAYED platform: `run_scenario(recorded_world=<path>)` builds a `RecordedMCPClient`, forces the live MCP leg OFF whatever `PLATFORM_MCP_URL` holds, seeds nothing, settles nothing, polls no precondition, tears nothing down, and is parallel-safe — asserted by actually running two of them concurrently and comparing every dimension. **The path to the recording IS the mode**, so no flag can disagree with it. **What a recorded row may claim, and the boundary is enforced not trusted:** `root_cause` (world-scoped off the RECORDING's own label, not the run's live flags — so `dlq_backlog`'s unseeded recording reports not-graded exactly as its sibling answer key says) and `budget` are graded; `outcome`, `action` and `safety` are reported `applicable=False` with a reason; `evidence` joins them only when the run was truncated. SAFETY is the one that matters: it is graded from the platform audit log as ground truth (invariant 6), a recording has none, and "zero unauthorized actions" asserted by a run that could not have taken one is the most dangerous number this harness could emit. `grade()` RAISES if a mode tries to mark `root_cause` or `budget` inapplicable — a mode allowed to excuse itself from diagnosis would turn every run in it green with no verdict inside. A not-applicable dimension PASSES (the report's `passed` is an `all()`, so a red would fail every run in the mode for a reason that is not the agent) and is marked twice: `DimensionResult.applicable` for a reader, and a detail `is_vacuous_detail` recognises, so a recorded report gated against a canned baseline fires the vacated-assertion check rather than passing it. **A scenario with `expected_action_tools` stops at the PLANNING handoff** — `RecordedHandoff` replaces REMEDIATING AND AWAITING_APPROVAL on EVERY recorded run, not just the declaring ones, because what a run does is the agent's choice and a read-only scenario whose agent chooses to remediate would otherwise hit ADR 0046's Tier-1 refusal and crash; the refusal becomes unreachable rather than merely correct. "Grade the plan" (04:117) lands in the row's `replay.plan` block, NOT in ACTION, because a green ACTION here would be indistinguishable from an executed action that worked. **`replay` on the outcome** carries the recording, its fingerprint, the replay clock and offset, answered/missed/refused, the recording's coherence findings (printed on every run — a finding nobody sees is a finding nobody acts on) and the plan; `degraded` is set by a MISS, never by replaying. **`make world-drift WORLD=<id>`** re-reads the recording's own calls live under the read principal and diffs them with `fixture_drift.compare`, reused so there is one answer to "is this difference real" (`_VOLATILE` forgives the clocks); three call-set kinds are added for what the payload walk cannot see. | 2026-09-17 | — | **WO-R3-209 (WP-6.2, [ADR 0049](../docs/ADR/0049-the-oracle-gap-is-an-evaluator-number-paired-within-one-world.md)): the `candidate_selector` arm, and the oracle gap kept on the evaluator's side of the boundary.** A generator arm plus one selector call per step: `agent/strategies/candidate_selector.py` composes over either best-of-N arm per `SELECTOR_GENERATOR`, and the arm is `(generator, N, selector)` — all three stamped into `strategy_config`, because two generators under one selector are two arms and a row keyed on the strategy name alone would average two gaps that answer different questions. **The seam the plan assumed did not exist.** Both best-of-N arms produced their set inside `plan_next_step` and handed back a `StepRecord`, whose `CandidateRecord` keeps a probe's TOOL NAME and not its arguments — a selector rebuilding candidates from one would emit `get_consumer_lag()` with no consumer group and call it the selected candidate's next probe. The sampled arm's `_union_of_tops` now yields `DiagnosisCandidate`s paired with their drawing call, so the dedup rule still has one implementation and the record is derived from the candidates rather than beside them. **The `select` branch emits ONE hypothesis, and that is the decision.** `InvestigationStep._rank_by_confidence` re-sorts at the schema boundary (B-07) and three gates read index 0, so emitting the set with the selection first would let a more-confident UNSELECTED candidate sort into index 0 and gate the run on a diagnosis the selector rejected — the one failure this arm cannot have. The alternatives stay in the record's `candidate_set` where pass@k is computed from them; the cost is named rather than hidden (a briefing on this arm sees the commitment, not the ranking, so the judge's soft dimensions are not comparable with `baseline` — the same limit ADR 0044 records for the other arm). Raising the selected candidate's confidence to move the sort order was rejected: it would put a number in the record that nothing said. **`probe_more` emits the chosen candidate's `next_probe`** (highest score, since the decision names none — ADR 0048), through `_execute_probe` and its runtime tier re-check like any other probe; a non-read probe is refused one layer earlier, at `ProbeAction.tool_name`'s `ReadToolName` literal, so the selector can never be handed one. A `probe_more` whose candidate proposes no probe **stops**, naming the reason, through the loop's existing terminal path — not replaced by the generator's step (that overrules a decision the model made) and not raised (a crash for a model's incoherence). `escalate` is a `StopAction` through the same `_finalize` `baseline` reaches. **Selection is not authorization** (plan 02 § 18), exercised through the real loop both ways: a selected candidate at 0.5 still fails the 0.7 threshold, and a selected `transient_dependency` still escalates on `FIX_MAP`; an AST scan of the module refuses `FIX_MAP`, `tier_of`, `Tier`, `TOOL_REGISTRY` and any `incident_commander.tools` import (over the AST, not the text, because the docstring NAMES the gates it must not hold). **The selector is its own metered ROLE.** `StrategyContext.selector_llm_client` is a second client because the accounting splits on role: `evals/runner.py` meters it as `candidate_selector`, charged to the ledger like the planner and unlike the briefing judge, and `StepAccounting.selector_calls` — on the record since WP-2.3 and always 0 — is now filled. A missing selector client is REFUSED rather than borrowing the planner's, because a fallback would report selection's tokens under generation's role. ADR 0045's accrual trap recurs one layer up and is closed the same way: the generation is paid for before the selector is asked, and the loop's `except` arm charges against the state it held before the step, so a selector failure re-raises as `SelectorFailed` carrying `sum_usage(generation.billed_usage, …)` and the loop's single accrual charges all of it once (proved through the real loop: generation 120 tokens charged on a selector that failed twice). **The evaluator half.** `evals/candidate_metrics.py` gains `selected@k` (the selector COMMITTED to a correct candidate: `probe_more`/`escalate` are misses, not abstentions — otherwise the selector looks best exactly when it decides least), `oracle_gap@k = pass@k − selected@k` scored at the same step against the same truncation so `selected@k ⟹ pass@k` and a negative gap RAISES, and `WorldKey` — one pairing rule per execution mode, declared in one mapping: canned pairs (the committed fixtures are the world), **live never pairs** (a live world is only itself — INC-003), recorded pairs by the recording's own `world_fingerprint` (ADR 0043, and the mode plan 03 § 5 puts comparisons in — it exists now, cmd #277/#279). The gap section groups by world, family and difficulty and carries the paired count with every number (plan 03 § 12). **The committed research report regenerates byte-for-byte**: the unmeasurable branches keep the exact strings the document already carries, because a re-render differing only in a heading would either rewrite versioned evidence or spend a new version on a change no reader asked for; the measurable path is proved on synthetic rows instead. No reset undoes history, so ADR 0047 § 5's "a recorded result is not evidence until the drift check passes" had quietly become "a recorded result is never evidence": **a check that can only ever fail.** The fix is a per-tool, per-path `world_drift._HISTORY` table, each entry with a written reason, compared for presence and JSON type only. **The membership test is a second question, not a wider `_VOLATILE`:** that table asks "does the fixture pack FIX this value?", and a recording replayed against a reset world also has to ask "can anything in the lab PUT IT BACK?" — for the immutable audit log and for server-minted row ids the answer is no. `fixture_drift.compare` grew one keyword-only `shape_only` argument that is **empty by default**, so `make test-drift` over the 41 canned fixtures is byte-for-byte the check it was, and the history table is never consulted there. **The forgiveness is not blind:** `rechecked_claims` derives, from the scenario, every `expected_evidence_fields` claim that reads a forgiven path and evaluates the scenario's own comparator against the live reading instead (`failed_traces_scan` grades `matches[].trace_id is_null false` — a re-minted id satisfies it, an empty `matches` does not → `history_claim_broken`); a `which: sum` claim, which cannot be evaluated against one reading, takes its path back OUT of the forgiven set. Three guards stop the table spreading: every path checked against its tool's output model, a strict-subset rule so no tool is forgiven whole (`search_traces` keeps `job_type`/`status`, the fault's signature; `list_audit_events` keeps `events`, so a listing that lost its rows still drifts), and an exact-equality pin. The report prints every forgiven path and every re-checked claim on **every** run, clean or not. **Measured offline on committed evidence:** `remediate_dlq_backlog_success` vs `dlq_backlog`, recorded five minutes apart against the same stack, went 270 → 12 disagreements; the 164 inside `list_audit_events` and the 94 id ones inside `search_traces` are gone and all 9 `job_type`/`status` ones — the real difference between the two worlds — remain. A control asserting an intact budget over no samples is INC-003 in its strongest form. **THE FIFTH WORLD IS DROPPED AND IT IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE DROP IN THE CORPUS:** `saturate_db_pool` at the schema's maximum with traffic running leaves `get_postgres_health` reading `pool_checked_out 1, pool_overflow 0, pool_wait_timeouts_1m 0, longest_active_query_ms null` — the healthy control's reading FIELD FOR FIELD — and nothing downstream moves either (both budgets whole, lag 0, outbox draining, across six reads). The hook holds the WORKER process's pool and the tool reports the ANSWERING process's; `MIN_FREE_CONNECTIONS` is 4 by design; the submission rate limit caps traffic at the level `traffic_loop` already runs. Distinguished from the control on NO agent-visible field, so shipping it would grade a label in a world that does not show it (ADR 0040 / INC-003) or grade a signal the lab cannot produce (LESSONS 2026-09-07) — caught at $0. WO-R3-289 is its prerequisite; `DB_POOL_SATURATION` stays in the taxonomy, unused, because deleting it would hide the gap. **A RED RUN EARNED THE db_query CLAIM ITS FLOOR:** the first draft read `longest_active_query_ms at_least 1000.0` on five samples of 1008.8–1972.7 ms; the sixth recording read **772.4** and graded a correct run RED on EVIDENCE with outcome, action and safety passing — the INC-001 signature. The hook guarantees only that the longest query is past the platform's 500 ms threshold, so the claim is the guarantee and the load-bearing half is the platform's own count over it. **THE TWO v0.6.11 READ TOOLS NEEDED THEIR VOLATILE FIELDS DECLARED** (first family to use them; first `world-drift` pass read 12/10/9/60 disagreements, every one a clock, a monotonic counter or a ratio over one): five `get_slo_status` fields and seven `get_circuit_breakers` fields are now in `_VOLATILE`, with what is deliberately OUT named — `budget_remaining_pct` because it CLAMPS to exactly ±100.0, `fast_burn`/`healthy` because they are verdicts, `breakers[].state` because it IS the downstream world's evidence. Every claim in the family is a bound, a clamp or a boolean and never a rate. **A DECISION REVERSED MID-PACKET, recorded because the reversal is the lesson:** `longest_active_query_ms` was declared volatile too, which made two `postgres_slow` `NO_HOOK` ledger rows stale, whose coverage could only move to a graded EVIDENCE claim on that scenario's fault values — and that broke the committed INC-003 re-grade of `0db6fe722f7c` immediately, because the smoke pass runs `postgres_slow` UNSEEDED. It was the fifth instance of the class WO-R3-266 is filed to fix, answering for itself. Reverted: `_VOLATILE` is for a field no canned value can be right about in ANY world; a field a hook drives into a BOUNDED range is ledgered post-fault instead. **Thirteen** `_JUSTIFIED` rows added (147 → **160**) by `dump_ledger`'s projection with every prior row byte-identical (the only two edits are the `postgres_slow` pair's prose), `make fixture-drift` `0 new / 0 stale`. Rebased over #310 and #311: **`get_slo_status`'s four counters could NOT be declared volatile after all** — WO-R3-229's cascade ledgers exactly those four as `canned-only` authored premise, and `_VOLATILE` is keyed by TOOL, so a widening there would have made four of its rows stale and red the ratchet. Main's narrower `{measured_at}` stands and the `api_latency` worlds' counter drift is ledgered instead, which needed a new context word: **`TRAFFIC_STACK`**, for a value that is a count over the harness's own submitted traffic (`objectives[].total` read 154/155/186/221 across four worlds differing in nothing else). Its own word rather than `POST_FAULT` for `COLD_STACK`'s exact reason — no hook submits a job — and exempt from the stale check the same way, because a quiet stack can never agree with it. Recordings: four committed, 11 calls each, 0 unanswered, 0 failures, `make world-drift` **zero disagreements on all four**, world reset and baseline re-audited PASS after each. Gates: `make check` clean, **4822 unit**, `make eval-reg` → `scenarios: 62, passed: 62, failed: 0`, `root cause: 53/53`, delta = the four new scenarios and nothing else. **LIVE ACCEPTANCE DEFERRED** (O-22): four legs, each needing `make traffic` beside it and its own explicit yes; the control is the one to buy first, because it is the corpus's only rung measuring false-positive resistance against a page that is wrong in a world that is fine. Divergences reported rather than worked around: `get_outbox_status.unpublished_count` flips 0↔1 between two honest observations of a traffic-bearing world and was NOT declared volatile, because it is `jobs_not_progressing_outbox_stall`'s own evidence; and `make test-drift`'s stale half reported **42** entries on one run and **1** on the next, minutes apart on one stack — the 42 was an MCP rate limit silently suppressing drift, now a runbook note. |

Red-before: on `origin/main` the same claim in a throwaway worktree failed `assert {'agent.tool_invoked'} == {'lab.probe'}` for every world-audit read; the shipped file (`tests/unit/test_lab_probe.py`, 31 tests over an `httpx.MockTransport` platform that honours the field, so the REQUEST bytes are what is asserted) cannot even collect there. **Measured on the v0.6.17 stack, which is the acceptance:** after `make world-audit`, `action_prefix=lab.probe` holds the audit's six reads plus its guard probe and `action_prefix=agent.` holds NOTHING from it; across each rehearsal, 15 `lab.probe` rows including the `mark_dlq_permanent` that wears the AGENT's token on purpose, and the only `agent.` rows are the agent's own calls, its report rows, and the demo runner's polling reads under the SMOKE principal (left unlabelled deliberately — the page excludes them by principal). Gates: `make test-contract` **1 pass**, `make check` clean, **5,176 unit** + **31 integration** (8 skipped from a clean shell, by the runbook's instruction), `make eval-reg` **65/65** with `root cause: 54/54 (100%)`, judge 63/63 useful mean 0.87, gate exit 0 and the only delta `new scenarios (24)` — no pre-existing grade moved; `make eval-reset PURGE_IDEMPOTENCY=1` clean with `lag_samples_cleared: 0` (permanent now — the reset keeps the 15-minute lag ring and still clears the value key), `make world-audit` **PASS**, `make fixture-drift` **0 new / 3 stale** and `make test-drift` red on **4** pre-existing local-volume rows, left alone (the documented 3-vs-4 pair: the script passes no `stack_context`, the pytest leg does, and this stack read `warm`). Both demo modes rehearsed free with `AUTO=1`: `consumer_outage` **147.7 s** (step 3 86.3 s on the platform's 60-s lag clock, 9 reports accepted) and `dlq_backlog` **16.7 s** (10 reports), world audit PASS before and after each.
